**标题**: Grok 4 | SpaceXSI

# Grok 4

Grok 4 is the most intelligent model in the world. It includes native tool use and real-time search integration, and is available now to SuperGrok and Premium+ subscribers, as well as through the xAI API. We are also introducing a new SuperGrok Heavy tier with access to Grok 4 Heavy - the most powerful version of Grok 4.

Grok 4Scaling Up Reinforcement LearningNative Tool UseGrok 4 HeavyFrontier IntelligenceGrok 4 APIGrok 4 Voice ModeWhat’s Next

## [Scaling Up Reinforcement Learning](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#scaling-up-reinforcement-learning)

With Grok 3, we scaled next-token prediction pretraining to unprecedented levels, resulting in a model with unparalleled world knowledge and performance. We also introduced Grok 3 Reasoning, which was trained using reinforcement learning to think longer about problems and solve them with increased accuracy. During our work on Grok 3 Reasoning, we noticed scaling trends that suggested it would be possible to scale up our reinforcement learning training significantly.

For Grok 4, we utilized Colossus, our 200,000 GPU cluster, to run reinforcement learning training that refines Grok's reasoning abilities at pretraining scale. This was made possible with innovations throughout the stack, including new infrastructure and algorithmic work that increased the compute efficiency of our training by 6x, as well as a massive data collection effort, where we significantly expanded our verifiable training data from primarily math and coding data to many more domains. The resulting training run saw smooth performance gains while training on over an order of magnitude more compute than had been used previously.

### Humanity's Last Exam

Deep expert-level benchmark at the frontier of human knowledge

### State of the art

Full set (April 3, 2025) with Python and Internet tools

## [Native Tool Use](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#native-tool-use)

Grok 4 was trained with reinforcement learning to use tools. This allows Grok to augment its thinking with tools like a code interpreter and web browsing in situations that are usually challenging for large language models. When searching for real-time information or answering difficult research questions, Grok 4 chooses its own search queries, finding knowledge from across the web and diving as deeply as it needs to craft a high-quality response.

We also trained Grok to use powerful tools to find information from deep within X. Grok can use advanced keyword and semantic search tools and even view media to improve the quality of its answers.

## [Grok 4 Heavy](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#grok-4-heavy)

We have made further progress on parallel test-time compute, which allows Grok to consider multiple hypotheses at once. We call this model Grok 4 Heavy, and it sets a new standard for performance and reliability. Grok 4 Heavy saturates most academic benchmarks and is the first model to score 50% on Humanity's Last Exam, a benchmark "designed to be the final closed-ended academic benchmark of its kind."

## [Frontier Intelligence](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#frontier-intelligence)

Grok 4 represents a leap in frontier intelligence, setting a new state-of-the-art for closed models on ARC-AGI V2 with 15.9% (nearly double Opus's ~8.6%, +8pp over previous high). On the agentic Vending-Bench, it dominates with $4694.15 net worth and 4569 units sold (averages across 5 runs), vastly outpacing Claude Opus 4 ($2077.41, 1412 units), humans ($844.05, 344 units), and others. Grok 4 Heavy leads USAMO'25 with 61.9%, and is the first to score 50.7% on Humanity's Last Exam (text-only subset), demonstrating unparalleled capabilities in complex reasoning through scaled reinforcement learning and native tool use.

## [Grok 4 API](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#grok-4-api)

The Grok 4 API empowers developers with frontier-level multimodal understanding, a 256,000 context window, and advanced reasoning capabilities to tackle complex tasks across text and vision. It integrates real-time data search across X, the web, and various news sources via our newly launched live search API, enabling up-to-date, accurate responses powered by native tool use. With enterprise-grade security and compliance—including SOC 2 Type 2, GDPR, and CCPA certifications—the API ensures robust protection for sensitive applications. Grok 4 is coming soon to our hyperscaler partners, making it easier for enterprises to deploy at scale for innovative AI solutions.

## [Grok 4 Voice Mode](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#grok-4-voice-mode)

Speak with Grok in our upgraded Voice Mode, which features enhanced realism, responsiveness, and intelligence. We introduce a serene, brand-new voice and redesign conversations to make them even more natural.

And now, Grok can see what you see! Point your camera, speak right away, and Grok pulls live insights, analyzing your scene and responding to you in real-time from within the voice chat experience. We are proud to present this model trained in-house, with our state-of-the-art reinforcement learning framework and speech compression techniques.

Enable video during your voice chat and Grok will look at what it sees when talking to you.

## [What’s Next](https://x.ai/news/grok-4#whats-next)

xAI will continue scaling reinforcement learning to unprecedented levels, building on Grok 4's advancements to push the boundaries of artificial intelligence. We plan to expand the scope from verifiable rewards in controlled domains to tackling complex real-world problems, where models can learn and adapt in dynamic environments. Multimodal capabilities will see ongoing improvements, integrating vision, audio, and beyond for more intuitive interactions. Overall, our focus remains on making models smarter, faster, and more efficient, as we drive toward systems that truly understand and assist humanity in profound ways.


===== https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1 =====
**标题**: GitHub - xai-org/grok-1: Grok open release · GitHub

# Grok-1

[](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1#grok-1)

This repository contains JAX example code for loading and running the Grok-1 open-weights model.

Make sure to download the checkpoint and place the `ckpt-0` directory in `checkpoints` - see [Downloading the weights](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1#downloading-the-weights)

Then, run

```
pip install -r requirements.txt
python run.py
```

to test the code.

The script loads the checkpoint and samples from the model on a test input.

Due to the large size of the model (314B parameters), a machine with enough GPU memory is required to test the model with the example code. The implementation of the MoE layer in this repository is not efficient. The implementation was chosen to avoid the need for custom kernels to validate the correctness of the model.

# Model Specifications

[](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1#model-specifications)

Grok-1 is currently designed with the following specifications:

- **Parameters:** 314B
- **Architecture:** Mixture of 8 Experts (MoE)
- **Experts Utilization:** 2 experts used per token
- **Layers:** 64
- **Attention Heads:** 48 for queries, 8 for keys/values
- **Embedding Size:** 6,144
- **Tokenization:** SentencePiece tokenizer with 131,072 tokens
- **Additional Features:**
  
  - Rotary embeddings (RoPE)
  - Supports activation sharding and 8-bit quantization
- **Maximum Sequence Length (context):** 8,192 tokens

# Downloading the weights

[](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1#downloading-the-weights)

You can download the weights using a torrent client and this magnet link:

```
magnet:?xt=urn:btih:5f96d43576e3d386c9ba65b883210a393b68210e&tr=https%3A%2F%2Facademictorrents.com%2Fannounce.php&tr=udp%3A%2F%2Ftracker.coppersurfer.tk%3A6969&tr=udp%3A%2F%2Ftracker.opentrackr.org%3A1337%2Fannounce
```

or directly using [HuggingFace 🤗 Hub](https://huggingface.co/xai-org/grok-1):

```
git clone https://github.com/xai-org/grok-1.git && cd grok-1
pip install huggingface_hub[hf_transfer]
huggingface-cli download xai-org/grok-1 --repo-type model --include ckpt-0/* --local-dir checkpoints --local-dir-use-symlinks False
```

The code and associated Grok-1 weights in this release are licensed under the Apache 2.0 license. The license only applies to the source files in this repository and the model weights of Grok-1.


===== https://x.ai/news/grok-os =====
**标题**: Open Release of Grok-1 | SpaceXSI

# Open Release of Grok-1

We are releasing the weights and architecture of our 314 billion parameter Mixture-of-Experts model Grok-1.

* * *

Open Release of Grok-1Model Details

**We are releasing the base model weights and network architecture of [Grok-1](https://x.ai/news/grok), our large language model. Grok-1 is a 314 billion parameter Mixture-of-Experts model trained from scratch by xAI.**

This is the raw base model checkpoint from the Grok-1 pre-training phase, which concluded in October 2023. This means that the model is not fine-tuned for any specific application, such as dialogue.

We are releasing the weights and the architecture under the Apache 2.0 license.

To get started with using the model, follow the instructions at [github.com/xai-org/grok](https://github.com/xai-org/grok).

## [Model Details](https://x.ai/news/grok-os#model-details)

- Base model trained on a large amount of text data, not fine-tuned for any particular task.
- **314B** parameter Mixture-of-Experts model with 25% of the weights active on a given token.
- Trained from scratch by xAI using a custom training stack on top of JAX and Rust in October 2023.

===== https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot =====
**标题**: Introducing Grok Bot | SpaceXSI

# Introducing Grok Bot

Grok Bot is your team of always-on agents. They have their own computer, work inside tools and apps like you do, and keep working 24/7.

[Download for Windows](https://api2.cursor.sh/updates/download/stable/win32-x64/grok-bot-cf55d121b6d17368)[Contact sales](https://cursor.com/contact-sales?product=grok-bot)

Introducing Grok BotHow Grok Bot is differentHow the SpaceXSI team is using Grok Bot

0:00 / 0:00

Today we’re launching Grok Bot, AI teammates you can give real work to.

Bots have their own computer. They sign into the tools you already use and work across apps, inboxes, and more. They finish jobs end to end, and only come back when something needs your approval.

You can message them like a colleague and hand off the work. They remember conversations, learn how you like things done, and get sharper the more you work together.

We built Grok Bot as an internal prototype, and it took off across the company. Teams created Bots to handle sales outbound, marketing campaigns, office operations, bug fixes, and more. Today we’re excited to open it up to the rest of the world.

Grok Bot is in beta and available today for SuperGrok, SuperGrok Plus, and SuperGrok Heavy; Cursor Pro, Pro+, and Ultra; and Cursor Teams Standard and Premium subscribers on desktop and iOS. Grok Bot comes with its own usage, separate from your Grok and Cursor plans, so anything you hand off to a Bot won’t count against your existing usage. [Download the app](https://x.ai/bot#download), create your first teammate, and start handing off work. Enterprise users can join a waitlist for future access.

## [How Grok Bot is different](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#how-grok-bot-is-different)

### [A computer of its own](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#a-computer-of-its-own)

Early access users say Grok Bot feels less like prompting an agent, and more like giving work to a highly capable teammate. Bots share a computer of their own in the cloud, so jobs do not stall when you step away. They can sign in and work across apps, tools, and websites, including platforms with no clean API or MCP, and come back with the work finished.

Inside SpaceXSI, that already looks like real, multi-step coordinated jobs being handled: a sales Bot updating the CRM with call transcript notes and drafting follow-ups, an ops Bot seating new hires and processing invoices received in Gmail, and an engineering Bot reproducing a bug in the product UI, filing the ticket, and handing the fix off to a debugging Bot.

> There is a huge difference between 90% done and 100% done. Most AI gets you almost there. Grok Bot can finish the swing, because the work lands where a human would put it, in the actual tool.

— Roman, Product

### [Message it like a teammate](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#message-it-like-a-teammate)

It is easy to get started with Grok Bot. You can message a Bot from your phone or desktop, the way you would text someone on your team, and pick up the same thread later on either surface.

Other AI tools may ask you to set up and build workflows and routines first. With Grok Bot, simply message a Bot to take on a task and it gets it done.

> There wasn’t anything to learn. It was just like bringing on a coworker. No automations to set up, no product quirks, no intricate naming. You’re just chatting with a friend.

— Fiona, Community

### [Work with many Bots at once](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#work-with-many-bots-at-once)

People inside SpaceXSI often run multiple Bots in parallel, with one to manage the others. A chief of staff sits on top, with a specialist for each lane: inbox management, expenses, recruiting, bug fixes, or operations. Instead of multiple agents you have to manage, Grok Bot gives you a small team that can work in parallel so you’re not the middleman.

Bots can independently message each other and share context in threads. When projects overlap, they stay aligned on the same account or project without requiring you to paste notes between chats.

You can also place Bots in a group chat where they can coordinate on their own. They pass work, assign ownership, and only pull you in for judgment calls.

> Working with Grok Bot feels like I have eight arms like an octopus, with every arm in concert with the others, each performing the task the way I would.

— Vincent, Growth

### [Show a Bot how it’s done](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#show-a-bot-how-its-done)

The best way for a Bot to learn your workflow is to ask it to follow along the next time you do a job. It watches the steps and remembers how you like the work done.

It saves your workflow as a routine, takes your corrections, and runs it on its own next time, so you can hand off the same multi-step process without re-explaining it again.

> I showed Grok Bot a workflow once and now I just fully trust it to run forever. I feel like I’m 2-3x more efficient because it does it without me verifying and reviewing.

— Bennett, Sales

### [Trust your Bots with more over time](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#trust-your-bots-with-more-over-time)

Bots are teammates that get sharper over time. They keep context on how you like work done. After a few tasks, they pick up your voice, your edge cases, and know when to ping versus keep going.

They can follow up on threads you dropped, nudge a stalled handoff, and pick work back up from previous conversations.

Over time they become more proactive, picking up work before you need to ask and knowing when something needs your attention.

> When I first started, I was checking in on them every 15 minutes and micromanaging the Bots to the point where they asked me why I kept asking so many questions.
> 
> Now I let it do its thing and it’s just gotten better with time.

— Emma, Operations

## [How the SpaceXSI team is using Grok Bot](https://x.ai/news/introducing-grok-bot#how-the-spacexsi-team-is-using-grok-bot)

A few different ways we have been using Grok Bot — pick a team, and open an example to see the run behind it:

SalesGrowth and MarketingOperations and FinanceProductEngineeringCommunity

Sales

### Sales outbound

A sales outbound Bot researches accounts overnight, scores contacts with intent, drafts email and LinkedIn in each seller’s voice, and readies an inbox of personalized drafts to approve.

Sales

### Demo readiness

A demo Bot spins up and checks the environment overnight, fixes broken seeds and stale data in the product UI, and drops a ready checklist before calls.

Sales

### Pipeline ops

A Bot keeps CRM and org-chart hygiene clean, flags stalls and commit risk, and lands a Monday scoreboard so people start the week oriented.

Sales

### Account follow-up

Account teams keep customers warm and response times low. They update CRM notes, send product updates, file support tickets, and sync next steps where the team already works.


===== https://x.ai/news/designing-grok-bot =====
## 简要结论

已从给定内容中抽取出可确认的产品/交互架构信息。核心架构是：**以 Bots 作为持久化主体，而不是以 chat sessions 作为主体**；把 `sessions`、`context windows`、`sandboxes`、`permissions`、`automations` 等底层概念藏在界面之后；用 **Bot roster、avatar presence、Bot’s own computer、heterogeneous transcript、Routines、per-Bot memory、account-level Tools/Skills、coordinating Bots / Group chats** 来支撑“持续存在的代理”。

**关键限制**：给定内容中没有文章发布日期，也没有低层基础设施规格；`VM`、`container`、具体 `sandbox` 实现、存储 schema、权限矩阵、审批状态机、调度器/事件总线实现均未被明确描述。文中只有 `sandboxes`、`permissions`、`runtime`、`own computer`、`shell`、`computer use` 等概念性表述。

## 1. 精确标题、日期与原文小节标题

### 精确标题

- **页面标题（给定元数据）**：`Designing Grok Bot for a world of persistent agents | SpaceXSI`
- **文章 H1 标题**：`Designing Grok Bot for a world of persistent agents`

### 日期

- **发布日期**：**未在提供的网页内容中出现**。没有 `date`、`published`、`updated` 或类似发布信息。
- 注意：文中出现的 `7:34 PM`、`Yesterday`、`Tuesday`、`Every day at 8:00 AM`、`Every Monday at 9:00 AM` 等是 UI 示例、相对时间或调度/触发器文案，**不是文章发布日期**。

### 原文小节标题清单

- 无标题导语 / 开头摘要
- `Rethinking the primitives`
- `From chat history to a Bot roster`
- `Presence as interface`
  - `Who is this`
  - `What are they doing`
  - `How much do I need to know`
- `Their computer, not yours`
- `The shape of information`
- `Organizing intelligence`
- `Work that keeps moving`
- `The disappearing interface`

---

## 2. 总体设计目标与架构方向

**章节**：无标题导语 / 开头摘要

文章一开始把问题定义为：传统 AI 界面围绕用户操作的 chat session，而 Grok Bot 要面向“跨会话持续存在、能自行承担责任”的 agent。

> “How we designed Grok Bot for agents that persist beyond a single session — from a chat history to a Bot roster, presence, a computer of the Bot’s own, and work that starts without a prompt.”

> “When we started designing Grok Bot, one of the central questions was how the interface should shape the relationship between user and agent. Most AI interfaces are organized around a chat session the user operates. Each session begins with setup, unfolds as the user looks on, and ends when the conversation stops.”

> “We wanted to design for an agent that persists beyond any one session and can carry responsibility on its own. That meant reconsidering some of the basic objects and signals of the interface, including what belongs in the sidebar, how an agent shows progress, and when its work should become visible.”

**架构含义**：

- 从“用户启动并观看一个会话”转向“用户维护一个持续 agent”。
- 需要重新设计：
  - sidebar 的主对象；
  - agent 如何显示进度；
  - 工作何时可见；
  - 工作何时不需要用户 prompt 即可开始。

---

## 3. `Rethinking the primitives`：对象模型与隐藏底层概念

**章节**：`Rethinking the primitives`

作者先列出 AI 产品积累的词汇，并明确指出：把这些概念都暴露给用户会增加理解负担。

> “AI products have accumulated a large vocabulary in a short time. Chats, sessions, models, context windows, memories, system prompts, projects, skills, connectors, agents, tools, sandboxes, permissions, and automations all describe real parts of these systems.”

> “But exposing each one as a separate product concept asks users to understand more than they need to. We started by asking which concepts a person actually needs in order to work with an agent.”

作者保留的五个产品级对象：

> “We kept coming back to five:”

> “1. **Bots** are persistent agents with their own identity, memory, runtime, and tools.”

> “2. **Chats** are the conversational interface for working with a Bot.”

> “3. **Prompts** give a Bot context or instructions. They can be used once, saved as Skills, or triggered automatically as Routines.”

> “4. **Tools** let Bots access information and take action through software, APIs, connectors, the shell, or computer use.”

> “5. **Artifacts** are the documents, designs, code, data, and other durable outputs that Bots create or modify.”

> “Everything else could remain beneath the interface until the user had a reason to care about it. The next question was which of these five objects should organize the product.”

### 抽取出的架构边界

| 对象 | 架构含义 |
|---|---|
| `Bots` | 持久化 agent，拥有 `identity`、`memory`、`runtime`、`tools` |
| `Chats` | 与 Bot 协作的对话界面，不是顶层组织单位 |
| `Prompts` | 一次性指令、保存为 `Skills`、或自动触发为 `Routines` |
| `Tools` | 通过 `software`、`APIs`、`connectors`、`shell`、`computer use` 访问信息与执行动作 |
| `Artifacts` | `documents`、`designs`、`code`、`data` 等 durable outputs |
| 其余概念 | `sessions`、`models`、`context windows`、`system prompts`、`projects`、`sandboxes`、`permissions`、`automations` 等被隐藏到界面之下，直到用户需要关注 |

**与 sandboxes / permissions 相关的明确信息**：

- `sandboxes` 和 `permissions` 只在“AI 产品词汇”列表中出现。
- 作者明确说这些属于可以“remain beneath the interface”的内容。
- 文中**没有**说明 sandbox 隔离、VM/container、OS、网络策略、权限 scope、credential 隔离或审批后端实现。

---

## 4. `From chat history to a Bot roster`：chat history vs Bot roster

**章节**：`From chat history to a Bot roster`

作者明确把 `Chats` 定义为可丢弃的，而把 `Bots` 定义为持续对象。

> “Chats are disposable. We start a conversation to solve a problem. It gets pushed down the sidebar. A week later, we start another one. You rarely go back beyond the most recent five.”

> “That behavior is perfectly reasonable when the unit of interaction is a question. It becomes strange when the thing on the other side of the interaction is supposed to know you, remember previous work, and take responsibility over time.”

> “So the main objects in Grok Bot are Bots, not conversations. A Bot has a name. It has an avatar and a title. It remembers its conversations with you. It has its own computer and tools. When you come back tomorrow, you are coming back to the same Bot.”

### 架构结论

- **旧模型**：聊天历史按时间下沉到 sidebar，用户通常只看最近几条。
- **新模型**：sidebar / 产品主对象是 **Bot roster**。
- **Bot 的持久属性**：
  - `name`
  - `avatar`
  - `title`
  - 记住与用户的 conversations
  - 自己的 `computer`
  - 自己的 `tools`
  - 第二天回来仍是同一个 Bot

### UI 示例中的 Bot roster / 状态片段

开头 UI 示例出现的 sidebar / roster 相关文本包括：

- `Search`
- `Kenny` — `7:34 PM` — `Need your yes on the Friday all-hands deck.`
- `Justin` — `8 intros drafted — sitting in the CRM till you send.`
- `Luke` — `7:34 PM` — `Inbox's at 3. Two need a reply today.`
- `Website launch` — `11:18 AM` — `John: checkout's clean on staging, 3 bugs closed.`
- `John` — `Yesterday` — `Repro'd the checkout crash. Write-up's in the ticket.`
- `Keith` — `Acme's wobbling. Drafted a Thursday check-in.`
- `Tyler` — `9:04 AM` — `14 receipts in. Still missing your Uber from Tuesday.`
- `Manuel` — `2:20 PM` — `Launch post is live. First 200 impressions.`
- `Jenny` — `Tuesday` — `3 places in SoMa. The Folsom 2bed is the one.`
- `Chang` — `10:12 AM` — `Sourced 3. Skipped one already in your ATS.`
- `Plugins`
- `Peng Zheng`
- `Message Kenny`
- `Kenny’s screen`
- `Routines`

其中 `Project Acme` 下还展示了 Bot 内的历史 prompts / tasks：

- `Draft a follow-up to Acme after Friday’s call`
- `Rewrite the pricing one-pager`
- `What should I ask in the security review?`
- `Build a champion map from my call notes`
- `Practice the demo with hard objections`
- `Compare these three competitor decks for me`
- `Show more`

**解读**：这些 prompts/历史项位于 Bot 对象之内，而不是作为顶层 chat history 组织产品；这与 “main objects in Grok Bot are Bots, not conversations” 一致。

---

## 5. `Presence as interface`：presence、状态与可见性

**章节**：`Presence as interface`

作者把 presence 定义为界面需要同时回答三个问题：

> “Once a Bot was something you maintain over time rather than a session you start, the way Bots appear in the product had to answer three questions at once:”

1. `Who is this?`
2. `What are they doing?`
3. `How much do I need to know?`

### 5.1 `Who is this`：avatar 作为身份系统

**章节**：`Presence as interface` → `Who is this`

> “A roster only works if it can be scanned quickly. As the roster grows, we did not want people to have to read every name each time they opened the product. They should be able to recognize a Bot from its avatar almost peripherally.”

> “At the same time, we wanted to keep the avatars consistent enough to read as one system. We studied character systems across illustration, animation, games, and interface design, exploring everything from initials and emojis to pixel art, watercolor, claymorphism, Noritake-style line art, silhouettes, and identicons.”

> “Most approaches solved one side of the problem better than the other. Watercolor and clay gave individual Bots plenty of character but carried too much detail at sidebar scale. Simpler systems sat more naturally in the interface, but often left the Bots looking interchangeable.”

> “The system we landed on keeps the basic construction consistent, using simple shapes and expressive eyes, then introduces distinction through controlled variations and accessories. Each Bot remains recognizable at a glance without appearing to come from a different visual world.”

**架构/设计细节**：

- roster 必须可快速扫描。
- avatar 用于近乎“周边视觉”地识别 Bot。
- 探索过的风格：`initials`、`emojis`、`pixel art`、`watercolor`、`claymorphism`、`Noritake-style line art`、`silhouettes`、`identicons`。
- 最终方案：
  - 统一基础构造；
  - `simple shapes`；
  - `expressive eyes`；
  - 通过 `controlled variations` 和 `accessories` 区分个体。

### 5.2 `What are they doing`：avatar 承载生命周期状态

**章节**：`Presence as interface` → `What are they doing`

> “Once the avatar became the Bot’s identity, it was also the natural place to show state. A Bot may be idle, thinking, working, waiting, blocked, or done. We could have represented each state with a separate indicator, but that would have added another layer of UI for the user to interpret.”

> “Instead, we explored how much of the lifecycle the avatar itself could carry.”

> “At rest, the Bot is calm and slightly curious. When work arrives, it acknowledges the task. As work begins, it kicks into gear. Its motion changes again when it is waiting or needs help, then settles once the work is done. The avatar now shows what the Bot is doing as well as which Bot it is.”

原文还列出状态标签与动效系统：

- `Idle`
- `Working`
- `Waiting`
- `Blocked`
- `Thinking`
- `Done`
- `Avatar motion system`

**状态架构**：

- Bot 状态集合：`idle`、`thinking`、`working`、`waiting`、`blocked`、`done`。
- 状态不是用单独 indicator 表达，而是由 avatar motion 承载。
- 状态变化覆盖：
  - at rest / calm and slightly curious；
  - work arrives / acknowledges task；
  - work begins / kicks into gear；
  - waiting or needs help；
  - settles once work is done。

### 5.3 `How much do I need to know`：执行细节的可见性

**章节**：`Presence as interface` → `How much do I need to know`

作者描述了两种失败/不足方案：三个点信息太少；显示当前动作文字会让用户想看完整步骤。

> “A related design question was how much of the Bot’s execution to show. One approach would have been the standard “three animated dots” but that would have been too little information, making it hard for users to tell whether the Bot was working or stuck.”

> “We also tried showing a short written description of the Bot’s current action, but once people could see one step, they wanted to see the rest. User research showed us that they were asking for that detail mainly for reassurance that the Bot was still working and on the right track.”

> “In the final design, the avatar’s motion provides the first bit of reassurance by showing that the Bot is active. If someone wants to check what it is doing, they can hover to see its current action.”

UI 中出现的当前动作示例：

- `Searching the web`

**最终可见性策略**：

- 第一层：avatar motion 表示 Bot active，提供 reassurance。
- 第二层：hover 查看 `current action`。
- 不持续暴露完整执行细节，避免用户被拉入监督模式。

---

## 6. `Their computer, not yours`：Bot 自己的 computer、sandbox/VM 线索与访问级别

**章节**：`Their computer, not yours`

### 6.1 每个 Bot 有自己的 computer / runtime

> “Each Bot has its own computer, which it can use to browse the web, work with files, and run software. This created another interface problem. How visible should that computer be and when should the user be able to control it?”

与 `Rethinking the primitives` 中 `Bots` 的定义合并后，可确认的架构信息是：

- Bot 有 `runtime` 和 `tools`。
- Bot 有自己的 `computer`。
- 该 computer 可用于：
  - browse the web；
  - work with files；
  - run software。
- `Tools` 可通过 `software`、`APIs`、`connectors`、`shell`、`computer use` 访问信息和执行动作。

### 6.2 四种被探索的 computer 展现方式及其问题

> “We explored four arrangements:”

> “- **Floating window:** kept the computer easy to reach but covered the conversation.”

> “- **Side by side:** made the work continuously visible and encouraged users to watch it.”

> “- **Modal:** made checking in easy but treated the Bot’s workspace as a temporary interruption.”

> “- **Full screen:** gave the computer plenty of room but displaced the conversation entirely.”

> “The more prominent we made the computer, the more the product encouraged users to supervise it. We decided it should remain the Bot’s workspace, with the interface providing different levels of access as the user needed them.”

### 6.3 最终三层访问模型

> “The final design has three levels, which allow the user to enter the Bot’s workspace without being drawn into operating it:”

> “- **Status:** the title-bar icon turns purple while the computer is active.”

> “- **Preview:** opening it reveals a pinned side panel where the user can follow the work without leaving the conversation.”

> “- **Takeover:** when the Bot needs help, the user can open the computer full screen, take control, and then hand it back.”

| 级别 | 架构/交互含义 |
|---|---|
| `Status` | title-bar icon 变紫，表示 computer active |
| `Preview` | pinned side panel，用户可跟随工作但不离开 conversation |
| `Takeover` | Bot needs help 时，用户 full screen 接管、take control，然后 hand it back |

### 6.4 workspace 独立感

> “We also designed wallpapers that shift throughout the day, becoming lighter in the morning and darker at night. The detail gives the Bot’s computer its own sense of time and makes it feel separate from the user’s desktop.”

> “Dynamic wallpaper”

> “It is closer to working with a coworker than operating a remote machine. You can tell that they are working, glance at their screen when you need context, and sit down when something requires your help.”

### 6.5 sandboxes / VMs：明确可抽取与未明确部分

**明确出现**：

- `sandboxes`：只在 `Rethinking the primitives` 的词汇列表中出现。
- `runtime`：在 `Bots are persistent agents with their own identity, memory, runtime, and tools.` 中出现。
- `own computer`：每个 Bot 有自己的 computer。
- `shell`、`computer use`：作为 `Tools` 的 action surfaces。

**未明确出现**：

- 没有 `VM`、`virtual machine`、`container`、`Docker`、`hypervisor`、`OS image`、`filesystem isolation`、`network isolation`、`credential vault` 等低层描述。
- 没有说明 Bot computer 是否由 VM/container/sandbox 实现。
- 没有说明多 Bot 之间 computer 的隔离机制或资源配额。

---

## 7. `The shape of information`：heterogeneous transcript、inline widgets 与 action events

**章节**：`The shape of information`

### 7.1 早期失败：几乎全部用 prose，用户需要重组答案

> “Early versions of Grok Bot responded to almost every request with prose. It described a five-day forecast instead of showing one and narrated a set of tasks instead of laying them out as a board. The user then had to restructure the answer. This led us to treat the form of a response as part of the answer.”

> “To support this, we built inline cards and widgets into Grok Bot. A Bot can answer in prose when prose fits the information and use structured UI when it does not.”

**架构含义**：

- 回答的“形式”被视为答案的一部分。
- 支持 `inline cards` 和 `widgets`。
- 信息适合 prose 时用 prose；不适合时用 structured UI。
- 例子：five-day forecast 应展示；tasks 应作为 board。

### 7.2 inline chat widgets / 可操作对象

UI 示例：

- `New email`
- `Ready to send`
- `Frompeng@grokbot.app`
- `Tosarah@acme.com`
- `SubjectMoving Friday’s design review to 2 PM`
- `Send email`
- `Discard`
- `Send emailDiscard`
- `Inline chat widgets`

邮件正文示例包含：

> “Hi Sarah,”

> “Could we move Friday’s design review from 11 AM to 2 PM? A client call came up and I don’t want to rush our discussion.”

> “Thanks,”  
> “Peng”

**架构/审批含义**：`Ready to send` + `Send email` / `Discard` 表明动作对象可直接嵌入 transcript，并由用户显式发送或丢弃；这是一个可见的人工确认/操作点，但文中未描述其后端审批模型。

### 7.3 action events 直接进入 transcript

> “The same principle applies to actions. When a Bot creates a Routine, changes a setting, or messages another Bot, the event can appear directly in the transcript. The user can open it when there is more to inspect.”

UI 示例中的 transcript event：

- `9:41 AM`
- `Morning! Can you check in with everyone for me?`
- `On it — pinging the team for status now`
- `6 messages withKennyTylerandJenny`
- `All on track: Kenny shipped the landing page, Tyler sent this month's invoices, and Jenny booked next week's interviews. No blockers.`
- `Love it, can you do this every morning?`
- `Created RoutineMorning Briefing`
- `Done, your Morning Briefing will be here at 9:00 every day`

### 7.4 heterogeneous transcript

> “The result is a heterogeneous transcript in which conversation, system events, interactive objects, and visualizations share one timeline.”

**架构结论**：

- transcript 是统一时间线，承载：
  - conversation；
  - system events；
  - interactive objects；
  - visualizations。
- Bot 创建 `Routine`、修改 setting、给另一个 Bot 发消息，都会作为 event 出现在 transcript。
- 用户可打开 event 查看更多细节。

---

## 8. 状态与记忆持久化：per-Bot memory、account-level capabilities、Artifacts、transcript

**涉及章节**：`Rethinking the primitives`、`From chat history to a Bot roster`、`Presence as interface`、`Their computer, not yours`、`The shape of information`、`Organizing intelligence`、`Work that keeps moving`

### 8.1 持久化对象

| 层级 | 内容 | 原文依据 |
|---|---|---|
| Bot | `identity`、`memory`、`runtime`、`tools` | `Bots` 定义 |
| Bot | 记住与用户的 conversations | `It remembers its conversations with you.` |
| Bot | 自己的 computer 和 tools | `It has its own computer and tools.` |
| Bot | 跨天仍是同一个 Bot | `When you come back tomorrow, you are coming back to the same Bot.` |
| Artifacts | durable outputs：`documents`、`designs`、`code`、`data` | `Artifacts` 定义 |
| Memory / Routines | 属于 Bot | `Organizing intelligence` |
| Tools / Skills | 属于 account level | `Organizing intelligence` |
| transcript | 展示 what ran、review result、handle exception | `Work that keeps moving` |
| group chats | project/team shared context，同时每个 Bot retain specialized memory | `Organizing intelligence` |

### 8.2 状态生命周期

明确的状态与状态展示：

- Bot lifecycle states：`idle`、`thinking`、`working`、`waiting`、`blocked`、`done`
- avatar motion system 承载状态变化
- `Status`：title-bar icon purple when computer active
- `Ready to send`
- `Paused`（`Weekly team update`）
- `Created Routine`
- `Done`
- `No blockers`
- `still missing`、`Need your yes`、`till you send` 等 UI 片段表示等待用户或缺失输入

### 8.3 未明确的持久化实现

文中**没有**描述：

- 数据库、表结构、memory schema；
- state machine 的具体实现；
- event log / append-only log / transcript storage；
- vector store、embedding、RAG；
- checkpoint、snapshot、recovery；
- 多设备同步；
- 冲突解决或并发控制；
- retention / TTL / deletion policy。

---

## 9. `Organizing intelligence`：多 Bot 协调、上下文边界与角色分工

**章节**：`Organizing intelligence`

### 9.1 多 Bot 需要解决的核心问题

> “Once people create several Bots, the product also has to organize how those Bots work together. We needed to decide which context should belong to each role, how Bots should share context when their work overlaps, and how to coordinate them without turning the user into a dispatcher.”

### 9.2 Chief of Staff Bot 与 specialists

> “We saw one answer emerge as people created more Bots. Some made a Chief of Staff Bot responsible for coordinating several specialists. They could give direction to one Bot instead of checking each one and routing every task themselves.”

**架构含义**：

- 用户可创建 `Chief of Staff Bot` 负责协调多个 specialist Bots。
- 用户给一个 Bot 方向，避免逐个检查和手动路由任务。

### 9.3 角色上下文与反对“大一统 memory”

> “Giving Bots distinct roles also forced us to decide what each role should know. A legal Bot may need the history of an ongoing dispute, while a finance Bot may need years of financial records. Combining those histories into one large memory would make it harder to give each Bot the information relevant to its work.”

### 9.4 capabilities 与 context 的边界

> “Capabilities and context therefore follow different boundaries in Grok Bot. Tools and Skills live at the account level because many Bots may need to browse the web, work with documents, or send email. Memory and Routines belong to the Bot because they reflect what that particular role knows and does over time. Put another way, capabilities can be shared broadly while context remains with the role that needs it.”

| 类型 | 所属范围 | 原因 |
|---|---|---|
| `Tools` / `Skills` | account level | 多个 Bot 可能都需要 browse web、work with documents、send email |
| `Memory` / `Routines` | Bot level | 反映该角色长期知道什么、做什么 |
| 共享能力 | broadly shared | capabilities 可广泛共享 |
| context | role-specific | context 保留在需要它的角色那里 |

### 9.5 Group chats 与 shared context

> “Some work crosses those role boundaries. Group chats provide shared context for a project or team while allowing each Bot to retain its specialized memory. A designer, engineer, PM, and data scientist can work in the same conversation, hand work to one another, and share what the project requires.”

**架构含义**：

- `Group chats` 提供 project/team 的 shared context。
- 每个 Bot 仍保留 specialized memory。
- 示例角色：`designer`、`engineer`、`PM`、`data scientist`。
- 可在同一 conversation 中 `hand work to one another` 并共享项目所需信息。

### 9.6 拒绝 dashboards / assignment boards / explicit handoff controls

> “We considered adding dashboards, assignment boards, and explicit handoff controls to manage these groups. Each one gave the user more coordination work. Instead, coordinating Bots handle routine routing and bring the user in when a decision requires judgment.”

**协调架构结论**：

- 被考虑但未采用的机制：
  - `dashboards`
  - `assignment boards`
  - `explicit handoff controls`
- 原因：每一个都会增加用户 coordination work。
- 最终：`coordinating Bots` 处理 routine routing；只有 decision requires judgment 时才 bring the user in。

### 9.7 其他跨 Bot 启动/消息机制

**章节**：`The shape of information`、`Work that keeps moving`

> “When a Bot creates a Routine, changes a setting, or messages another Bot, the event can appear directly in the transcript.”

> “A prompt can start a session, but so can a schedule, an event, or another Bot.”

**架构含义**：

- Bot 可以给另一个 Bot 发消息。
- `another Bot` 可以启动 session。
- 跨 Bot 消息事件可进入 transcript。
- 用户不应成为 dispatcher。

### 9.8 明确限制

**章节**：`The disappearing interface`

> “We also set practical limits of roughly 50 Bots per account and six per group chat.”

- 大约 `50 Bots per account`
- `six per group chat`

---

## 10. `Work that keeps moving`：无需 prompt 启动的工作、Routines、scheduling/triggers

**章节**：`Work that keeps moving`

### 10.1 从 prompt-driven sessions 到 standing responsibility

> “Most agent sessions begin when a user sends a prompt. That leaves even a persistent Bot waiting for someone to activate it. Routines let users give a Bot a standing responsibility that runs on a schedule or in response to an event, such as watching an industry or preparing a briefing every morning. The user defines the work once, and the Routine activates the Bot when it needs to happen.”

### 10.2 Routines 从 secondary configuration 移入主界面

> “We initially treated Routines as secondary configuration. As they became more important to autonomous work, we moved them into the Bot’s main interface. The transcript shows what ran and gives the user a place to review the result or handle an exception.”

### 10.3 conversation 角色变化：session 可由多种来源启动

> “This also changes the role of conversation. A prompt can start a session, but so can a schedule, an event, or another Bot. Over time, more work may begin without the user being present at all.”

**架构结论**：

- 工作启动源包括：
  - user prompt；
  - schedule；
  - event；
  - another Bot。
- `Routines` 是 Bot 的 standing responsibility。
- 用户只定义一次，Routine 在需要时激活 Bot。
- transcript 展示运行过什么，并提供：
  - review the result；
  - handle an exception。

### 10.4 所有明确出现的 schedules / triggers

#### UI 与正文中出现的 Routines / schedule 示例

- `Morning briefing` — `Every day at 8:00 AM`
- `Inbox cleanup` — `Weekdays at 6:00 PM`
- `Weekly team update` — `Paused`
- `Done, your Morning Briefing will be here at 9:00 every day`
- `Created RoutineMorning Briefing`
- `Every day at 8:00 AM`
- `On weekdays at 8:00 AM`
- `Every Monday at 9:00 AM`
- `Monthly on the 1st at 8:00 AM`
- `Every 30 minutes`
- `On weekdays · 9:00 AM`

#### 正文中描述的自然语言调度/触发场景

> “such as watching an industry or preparing a briefing every morning”

#### `Work that keeps moving` 中列出的所有事件触发器原文字符串

- `Issue created in grokbot`
- `Issue any event in all projects`
- `Incident triggered on grokbot`
- `Incident any event on grokbot`
- `PR opened in grokbot`
- `PR merged in spacexai`
- `PR closed in acme`
- `New push to main`
- `Checks fail on PRs in grokbot`
- `Label bug added in grokbot`
- `Comment containing /fix in ops`
- `Review approved in grokbot`
- `Thread resolved in grokbot`
- `Workflow deploy.yml fails`
- `When webhook receives a POST`
- `New messages in #grokbot`
- `Reaction :eyes: added in #ops`
- `Channel created matching dev`
- `New messages in #design`
- `Issue created in Roadmap`
- `Issue status → In Review in Core`
- `At end of cycle for Team Core`
- `Issue status → Done in Design`
- `New messages in Design team`
- `Channel created in Design team`

**未明确的实现细节**：没有描述 scheduler、cron engine、queue、event bus、webhook authentication、retry、idempotency、backoff、dead-letter queue、rate limiting 或 trigger condition DSL。

---

## 11. Permissions 与 approvals：可确认的人工控制点

**涉及章节**：`Rethinking the primitives`、`From chat history to a Bot roster`、`Presence as interface`、`Their computer, not yours`、`The shape of information`、`Organizing intelligence`、`Work that keeps moving`

### 11.1 `permissions` 只作为隐藏底层词汇出现

> “Chats, sessions, models, context windows, memories, system prompts, projects, skills, connectors, agents, tools, sandboxes, permissions, and automations all describe real parts of these systems.”

> “Everything else could remain beneath the interface until the user had a reason to care about it.”

**含义**：`permissions` 被承认是系统真实部分，但被隐藏在界面之下；文中没有展开权限架构。

### 11.2 Tools 可执行动作，但未说明授权模型

> “Tools let Bots access information and take action through software, APIs, connectors, the shell, or computer use.”

**含义**：Bot 能访问信息并通过多种表面执行动作；没有给出 ACL、scope、OAuth、token、role、approval policy、审计日志等细节。

### 11.3 明确的人工审批 / 用户确认 UI 片段

| 场景 | 原文片段 | 含义 |
|---|---|---|
| roster 中等待 yes | `Need your yes on the Friday all-hands deck.` | Bot 等待用户明确同意 |
| 草稿等待发送 | `8 intros drafted — sitting in the CRM till you send.` | Bot 已起草，但发送动作留给用户 |
| 收件箱需回复 | `Inbox's at 3. Two need a reply today.` | 有需要用户处理的回复 |
| email widget | `Ready to send`、`Send emailDiscard` | 用户可发送或丢弃 |
| Bot needs help | `when the Bot needs help, the user can open the computer full screen, take control, and then hand it back.` | 用户接管 Bot computer 后可交还 |
| waiting / needs help 状态 | `waiting or needs help`、`blocked` | 执行状态中包含需要帮助或阻塞 |
| decision requires judgment | `bring the user in when a decision requires judgment` | 需要判断时引入用户 |
| exception handling | `review the result or handle an exception` | 用户可审阅结果或处理异常 |

### 11.4 未明确描述

文中没有明确给出：

- 权限模型或授权对象；
- tool-level permissions；
- per-Bot / per-account scope；
- approval workflow 状态机；
- 审批人、审批条件、审批持久化；
- 自动执行与人工执行的策略边界；
- audit trail；
- 凭证管理；
- 拒绝、撤销、回滚机制；
- 多用户审批。

---

## 12. 明确的 engineering trade-offs、失败方案与设计取舍

**涉及章节**：几乎每节；重点为 `Rethinking the primitives`、`Presence as interface`、`Their computer, not yours`、`The shape of information`、`Organizing intelligence`、`Work that keeps moving`、`The disappearing interface`

| 章节 | 取舍 / 失败 | 关键原句 |
|---|---|---|
| `Rethinking the primitives` | 暴露过多 AI 概念会增加用户理解负担；选择五个产品级对象，其余隐藏 | “But exposing each one as a separate product concept asks users to understand more than they need to.” / “Everything else could remain beneath the interface until the user had a reason to care about it.” |
| `From chat history to a Bot roster` | chat history 适合一次性问题，但不适合持续、有记忆、承担责任的 agent | “That behavior is perfectly reasonable when the unit of interaction is a question. It becomes strange when the thing on the other side of the interaction is supposed to know you, remember previous work, and take responsibility over time.” |
| `From chat history to a Bot roster` | 顶层对象从 conversations 改为 Bots | “So the main objects in Grok Bot are Bots, not conversations.” |
| `Presence as interface` → `Who is this` | 个性化 avatar 风格太复杂；简单风格太同质；最终采用统一构造 + 受控变化 | “Watercolor and clay gave individual Bots plenty of character but carried too much detail at sidebar scale. Simpler systems sat more naturally in the interface, but often left the Bots looking interchangeable.” |
| `Presence as interface` → `What are they doing` | 每种状态单独 indicator 会增加 UI 解释层；改用 avatar motion | “We could have represented each state with a separate indicator, but that would have added another layer of UI for the user to interpret.” |
| `Presence as interface` → `How much do I need to know` | 三个点信息太少，难判断 working/stuck | “One approach would have been the standard “three animated dots” but that would have been too little information, making it hard for users to tell whether the Bot was working or stuck.” |
| `Presence as interface` → `How much do I need to know` | 显示一步文字后会诱导用户想看全部步骤 | “We also tried showing a short written description of the Bot’s current action, but once people could see one step, they wanted to see the rest.” |
| `Presence as interface` → `How much do I need to know` | 用户研究显示详细步骤主要用于 reassurance；最终用 motion + hover current action | “User research showed us that they were asking for that detail mainly for reassurance that the Bot was still working and on the right track.” |
| `Their computer, not yours` | Floating window 覆盖 conversation | “kept the computer easy to reach but covered the conversation.” |
| `Their computer, not yours` | Side by side 让用户持续观看工作 | “made the work continuously visible and encouraged users to watch it.” |
| `Their computer, not yours` | Modal 把 workspace 当成临时打断 | “made checking in easy but treated the Bot’s workspace as a temporary interruption.” |
| `Their computer, not yours` | Full screen 完全挤掉 conversation | “gave the computer plenty of room but displaced the conversation entirely.” |
| `Their computer, not yours` | computer 越显眼，越鼓励监督；决定保持为 Bot workspace，按需求分级访问 | “The more prominent we made the computer, the more the product encouraged users to supervise it.” |
| `The shape of information` | 早期几乎全 prose，用户必须重组答案 | “The user then had to restructure the answer.” |
| `The shape of information` | response form 被视为 answer 的一部分；引入 inline cards/widgets 与 structured UI | “This led us to treat the form of a response as part of the answer.” / “we built inline cards and widgets into Grok Bot.” |
| `Organizing intelligence` | 合并所有历史成一个 large memory 会让相关性变差 | “Combining those histories into one large memory would make it harder to give each Bot the information relevant to its work.” |
| `Organizing intelligence` | capabilities 可 account-level 共享；context/memory/routines 保持 Bot-level | “capabilities can be shared broadly while context remains with the role that needs it.” |
| `Organizing intelligence` | dashboards、assignment boards、explicit handoff controls 增加用户协调负担，改由 coordinating Bots 路由 | “Each one gave the user more coordination work. Instead, coordinating Bots handle routine routing and bring the user in when a decision requires judgment.” |
| `Work that keeps moving` | 只由 prompt 启动会让 persistent Bot 仍等待用户激活 | “That leaves even a persistent Bot waiting for someone to activate it.” |
| `Work that keeps moving` | Routines 最初是 secondary configuration，后因 autonomous work 重要性移入主界面 | “We initially treated Routines as secondary configuration. As they became more important to autonomous work, we moved them into the Bot’s main interface.” |
| `The disappearing interface` | 后期大量工作是移除控件、视图选项、metadata，并设 practical limits | “We removed window and panel controls, computer-view options, and agent metadata. We also set practical limits of roughly 50 Bots per account and six per group chat.” |
| `The disappearing interface` | 每个决定用是否帮助 delegation、是否增加管理负担来评估 | “Did this help someone delegate, or did it give them one more thing to manage?” |
| `The disappearing interface` | 随模型改进，界面应从“操作 AI”向“委派 coworker”移动，并减少要求用户做的事 | “The line between operating an AI and delegating to a coworker keeps moving as models improve. Grok Bot reflects where we think it sits today. Designing Grok Bot from its earliest explorations through launch has been about finding that line and helping the interface change with it. As agents take on more responsibility, the interface should ask less of the person.” |

---

## 13. 明确未提及 / 不应从文中推断的内容

以下项目在提供内容中**没有明确架构说明**：

- **发布日期**：未提供。
- **作者**：未提供。
- **VM / sandbox 实现**：没有说明是否为 VM、container、microVM、browser sandbox、remote desktop 或其他隔离机制。
- **storage / persistence 实现**：没有说明 memory、transcript、Artifacts、Routines 的存储引擎或 schema。
- **permissions 实现**：没有权限矩阵、scope、RBAC/ABAC、OAuth、token、audit log。
- **approvals 实现**：没有审批状态机、审批策略、条件、撤销、回滚。
- **scheduler / event system 实现**：没有 cron engine、queue、event bus、webhook auth、retry、idempotency、rate limit。
- **multi-agent runtime 实现**：没有消息协议、任务队列、handoff schema、冲突解决、共享内存同步、并发控制。
- **tool execution 安全边界**：没有说明 `shell`、`computer use`、`APIs`、`connectors` 的隔离、白名单、审计或最小权限策略。
- **failure handling 后端**：只有 `blocked`、`needs help`、`handle an exception` 等 UI/产品层描述，没有重试、超时、补偿事务等工程细节。

**总体判断**：这是一篇偏产品/交互架构与 agent primitives 的文章；它清楚定义了对象边界、可见性层级、持久化语义、Routines 触发来源、多 Bot 协调原则和明确的取舍，但没有给出低层基础设施实现规格。


===== https://x.ai/news/grok-build-open-source =====
**标题**: Grok Build is Now Open Source | SpaceXSI

# Grok Build is Now Open Source

Explore the harness behind our coding agent and TUI.

We're open-sourcing Grok Build, SpaceXSI's coding agent and TUI. The source is now available on [GitHub](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build).

Publishing the code is the most direct way to build toward a robust and reliable harness. You can read the source to see exactly how it works, from context assembly to tool-call dispatch.

Open-sourcing also makes the harness easier to explore and extend: if you're working with skills, plugins, hooks, MCP servers, or subagents, the source is the definitive reference for how each is loaded and invoked.

Finally, Grok Build can now run fully local-first: compile it yourself, point it at your own local inference, and drive everything from your `config.toml`.

## [About the codebase](https://x.ai/news/grok-build-open-source#about-the-codebase)

The published source includes:

- The agent loop: how context is assembled, how model responses are parsed, and how tool calls are dispatched
- The tools: how the agent reads, edits, and searches code, and how it runs commands
- The terminal UI: rendering, input handling, plan review, and the inline diff viewer
- The extension system: skills, plugins, hooks, MCP servers, and subagents

Explore the source on [GitHub](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build).


===== https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build =====
## 简要结论

基于所给网页内容，可确认仓库为 **`xai-org/grok-build`**，项目名/CLI 为 **Grok Build (`grok`)**，第一方代码采用 **Apache License, Version 2.0**，README 明确说明源码主要是 **Rust**。页面提供了完整的发布版安装命令、源码构建要求、文档入口、仓库结构、开发命令、贡献限制和许可证说明。

但用户特别关心的 **本地推理配置**（`config.toml`、`base URL`、OpenAI-compatible endpoints）、**支持的 providers/models 清单**、**模型必须具备的能力**、**硬件要求**、以及 **当前版本号/Release tag**，在所给内容中**没有明确提供**。README 中也没有关于 local models 或 required model capabilities 的可逐字引用句子。以下内容仅基于所给页面文本；链接指向的 user guide、在线文档、changelog、`config.toml` 详情未展开，因此不能据此补充。

## 2. README 详细摘要

### 2.1 产品定位与能力

README 将 Grok Build 描述为 SpaceXAI 的终端 AI coding agent，运行为全屏 TUI，能理解代码库、编辑文件、执行 shell 命令、搜索网页、管理长任务；支持交互式、headless 脚本/CI，以及通过 ACP 嵌入编辑器。

原文：

> "**Grok Build** is SpaceXAI's terminal-based AI coding agent. It runs as a full-screen TUI that understands your codebase, edits files, executes shell commands, searches the web, and manages long-running tasks — interactively, headlessly for scripting/CI, or embedded in editors via the Agent Client Protocol (ACP)."

### 2.2 源码同步与 `SOURCE_REV`

README 说明该仓库包含 `grok` CLI/TUI 和 agent runtime 的 Rust 源码，并定期从 SpaceXAI monorepo 同步；根目录 `SOURCE_REV` 记录当前代码树对应的完整 monorepo commit SHA。

原文：

> "This repository contains the Rust source for the `grok` CLI/TUI and its agent runtime. It is synced periodically from the SpaceXAI monorepo."

> "A small `SOURCE_REV` file at the root records the full monorepo commit SHA for the version of the code present in this tree."

### 2.3 安装发布版二进制

README 说明 macOS、Linux、Windows 提供预构建二进制，并给出 `curl`、`irm` 安装命令以及 `grok --version` 验证命令；还指向 `https://x.ai/build/changelog` 查看每个 release 的修复、功能和改进。

### 2.4 从源码构建

要求包括：

- **Rust**：由 [`rust-toolchain.toml`](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/main/rust-toolchain.toml) 固定工具链，`rustup` 会在首次构建时自动安装。
- **DotSlash**：用于运行 [`bin/`](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/main/bin) 下的 hermetic tools，尤其是 [`bin/protoc`](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/main/bin/protoc)；要求构建前 `dotslash` 在 `PATH`。
- **protoc**：proto codegen 通过 DotSlash 解析 `bin/protoc`，或回退到 `PATH` / `$PROTOC` 中的 `protoc`。
- 构建主机：macOS 和 Linux 支持；Windows 从该源码树构建为 best-effort 且未测试。
- 首次启动会打开浏览器认证，认证指南位于 pager crate 的 user-guide 路径。

### 2.5 文档入口

README 给出：

- 在线文档：`https://docs.x.ai/build/overview`
- 随 pager crate 附带的 user guide：`crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/`
- user guide 覆盖：getting started、keyboard shortcuts、slash commands、configuration、theming、MCP servers、skills、plugins、hooks、headless mode、sandboxing 等。

### 2.6 仓库结构

README 的 Repository layout 说明：

| 路径 | 内容 |
|---|---|
| `crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager-bin` | Composition-root package；构建 `xai-grok-pager` binary |
| `crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager` | TUI：scrollback、prompt、modals、rendering |
| `crates/codegen/xai-grok-shell` | Agent runtime + leader/stdio/headless entry points |
| `crates/codegen/xai-grok-tools` | Tool implementations：terminal、file edit、search 等 |
| `crates/codegen/xai-grok-workspace` | Host filesystem、VCS、execution、checkpoints |
| `crates/codegen/...` | 其余 CLI crate closure：config、MCP、markdown、sandbox 等 |
| `crates/common/`、`crates/build/`、`prod/mc/` | Small shared leaf crates |
| `third_party/` | Vendored upstream source，Mermaid diagram stack |

重要提示：根 `Cargo.toml` 是生成文件，应视为只读，优先编辑各 crate 自己的 `Cargo.toml`。

### 2.7 开发、贡献与许可证

Development 部分给出按 crate 检查、测试、clippy、格式化命令。Contributing 明确不接受外部贡献。License 说明第一方 Apache 2.0，第三方/vendored code 保留原许可证，并列出 `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES`、crate-local notice、`third_party/NOTICE`。

---

## 3. 安装指令与源码构建指令

### 3.1 发布版安装

README 原文说明：

> "Prebuilt binaries are published for macOS, Linux, and Windows:"

安装命令逐字为：

```bash
curl -fsSL https://x.ai/cli/install.sh | bash   # macOS / Linux / Git Bash
irm https://x.ai/cli/install.ps1 | iex          # Windows PowerShell
grok --version
```

> "See the [changelog](https://x.ai/build/changelog) for the latest fixes, features, and improvements in each release."

### 3.2 DotSlash 安装检查

```bash
cargo install dotslash
# or: prebuilt packages — https://dotslash-cli.com/docs/installation/
/usr/bin/env dotslash --help   # sanity check
```

README 要求：

> "Install it and ensure `dotslash` is on your `PATH` **before** building"

### 3.3 构建与启动

```bash
cargo run -p xai-grok-pager-bin              # build + launch the TUI
cargo build -p xai-grok-pager-bin --release  # release binary: target/release/xai-grok-pager
cargo check -p xai-grok-pager-bin            # fast validation
```

> "The binary artifact is named `xai-grok-pager`; official installs ship it as `grok`. On first launch it opens your browser to authenticate — see the [authentication guide](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/main/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/02-authentication.md)."

### 3.4 开发命令

```bash
cargo check -p <crate>        # always target specific crates; full-workspace builds are slow
cargo test -p xai-grok-config # per-crate tests
cargo clippy -p <crate>       # lint config: clippy.toml at the repo root
cargo fmt --all               # rustfmt.toml at the repo root
```

## 6. 系统与硬件要求

### 6.1 明确给出的系统/构建要求

- 预构建二进制平台：`macOS, Linux, and Windows`
- 安装脚本适用：`macOS / Linux / Git Bash`、`Windows PowerShell`
- 源码构建支持主机：`macOS` 和 `Linux`；`Windows` 为 best-effort 且未测试
- 构建依赖：`Rust`、`DotSlash`、`protoc`
- Rust 工具链由 `rust-toolchain.toml` 固定
- 首次启动需浏览器认证
- 开发建议按 crate 执行，因为 `"full-workspace builds are slow"`

README 原文：

> "macOS and Linux are supported build hosts; Windows builds are best-effort and not currently tested from this tree."

### 6.2 硬件要求

**未提供**。页面没有 CPU、RAM、磁盘、GPU、VRAM、ARM/x86 最低配置、模型推理显存、并发线程要求等正式硬件要求。

commit message 中有一些硬件/平台相关修复线索，但不是正式要求：

- `"Fix aarch64 musl CLI SIGILL on CPUs without SHA-512"`
- `"Use ring on Windows ARM64 and build aws-lc without jitterentropy there"`
- `"Raise the runtime thread stack to 8 MB"`

## 7. MCP / skills / hooks / subagents 支持

### 总体判断

**有明确支持证据**，但 README 根文档主要给出文档入口和仓库结构；大量具体功能线索来自文件列表中的 commit message。

### 7.1 README 中的直接证据

README 说明 user guide 覆盖：

> "The user guide ships with the pager crate: [`crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/`](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/main/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide) — getting started, keyboard shortcuts, slash commands, configuration, theming, MCP servers, skills, plugins, hooks, headless mode, sandboxing, and more."

Repository layout 还提到：

> "`crates/codegen/...` The rest of the CLI crate closure (config, MCP, markdown, sandbox, ...)"

### 7.2 MCP 支持证据

commit message 中出现大量 MCP 相关功能：

- `"Surface structuredContent from MCP tool results to the model"`
- `"Tell the MCP server when a tool call is abandoned"`
- `"Render directly listed MCP tool calls with a label and their error"`
- `"Record how every MCP tool call ended"`
- `"Hand MCP tool results to the gateway as content blocks so images reach the model"`
- `"Show truthful headless MCP init statuses and drop the stale connecting reminder"`
- `"Show MCP tool search and MCP calls on the daemon path"`
- `"Upgrade the MCP SDK (rmcp) from 3.2.0 to 3.4.0"`
- `"Merge MCP server config from multiple sources"`
- `"Re-read an HTTP MCP bearer token from a file on every request"`
- `"Return MCP server details for settings reads"`

### 7.3 Skills 支持证据

- `"Document the skill read cap and session sweep"`
- `"Cap skill markdown reads and skill body injection at the read token cap"`
- `"Document extra_skill_dirs, hub approval, computer-use, and the read_file budget"`
- `"keep skills reachable when their name collides with a client builtin"`
- `"Re-copy agent skill definitions, take names from the full path, and honor disable-user-invocation"`

### 7.4 Hooks 支持证据

- `"security: fix SSRF bypass via HTTP redirect in hook runner"`
- `"Treat hooks in the signed requirements cache as managed policy"`
- `"Add allow_managed_hooks_only to skip every hook outside managed policy"`
- `"Deliver sandbox violation cards through the permission hook, with one replay of an allowed result"`

### 7.5 Subagents 支持证据

README 根文档的 user guide 列表没有显式写 "subagents"，但 commit message 反复出现相关功能：

- `"Remove the unclickable Done N completed Subagents dock row"`
- `"Show a background subagent as cancelled when its session is cut off"`
- `"Open the target subagent from the send_subagent_message row"`
- `"Show a child agent's transcript in the subagent view"`
- `"Background subagent rows finish when the child completes"`
- `"Approve shell commands from background subagents and daemon wakeup turns"`
- `"Bind a workspace task to one registry entry for the sub-agent"`
- `"Show \"Waiting on N subagent(s)\" while the parent turn is blocked"`
- `"Add a daemon parity lane and subagent spawning to end-to-end tests"`
- `"Re-expose subagent type on spawn so plugin and user agent types can be selected"`

> "A small `SOURCE_REV` file at the root records the full monorepo commit SHA for the version of the code present in this tree."

===== https://docs.x.ai/build/overview =====
**标题**: Grok Build: SpaceXSI's Coding Agent | SpaceXSI Docs

#### [Getting Started](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#getting-started)

# [Grok Build](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#grok-build)

[Create API key](https://console.x.ai/team/default/api-keys?utm_source=docs&utm_medium=referral&utm_campaign=build-overview&utm_content=article-api-key)[Meet grok-4.7](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-7)

Grok Build is a powerful and extensible coding agent. Use it via an interactive TUI, headlessly in scripts or bots, or through the Agent Client Protocol (ACP) in other apps.

The TUI provides a rich, mouse-interactive, fullscreen experience for coding with agents.

## [Install](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#install)

macOS / Linux / WSLWindows (PowerShell)

```
curl -fsSL https://x.ai/cli/install.sh | bash
```

```
irm https://x.ai/cli/install.ps1 | iex
```

## [Start an interactive session](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#start-an-interactive-session)

Bash

```
cd your-project
grok
```

On first launch, Grok opens a browser for authentication. In non-browser environments, use an API key:

Bash

```
export XAI_API_KEY="xai-..."
grok
```

Useful first prompts:

Text

```
Explain this repo.
@src/main.rs Walk me through this file.
```

## [Run headlessly](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#run-headlessly)

Bash

```
grok -p "Explain this codebase"
grok -p "Explain the architecture" --output-format streaming-json
```

Headless usage is ideal for scripts, automations, or integration into other apps.

## [Custom models](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#custom-models)

Grok supports any custom model. Add it to your user-level config file, `~/.grok/config.toml` (on Windows, `%USERPROFILE%\.grok\config.toml`):

Text

```
[model.my-model]
model = "model-id"
base_url = "https://api.example.com/v1"
name = "Display Name"
env_key = "API_KEY"

[models]
default = "my-model"
```

After updating `~/.grok/config.toml`, use `grok inspect` to see what Grok discovered in the current directory, including config sources, instructions, skills, plugins, hooks, and MCP servers, then pick the model in headless mode or in the TUI:

Bash

```
grok inspect
grok -p "Hello" -m my-model
```

You can also switch inside the TUI with `/model <name>`.

## [Use Grok 4.7 on the API](https://docs.x.ai/build/overview#use-grok-47-on-the-api)

[`grok-4.7`](https://docs.x.ai/developers/models/grok-4.7), SpaceXSI's latest model, is available directly on the xAI API. Drop it into your own agent loop, IDE integration, or coding tool.

JavaScriptPython (OpenAI)Bash Python

```
import { xai } from '@ai-sdk/xai';
import { generateText } from 'ai';

const { text } = await generateText({
  model: xai.responses('grok-4.7'),
  prompt: 'Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return a[a.length/2]}',
});

console.log(text);
```

```
from openai import OpenAI

client = OpenAI(
    api_key="<YOUR_XAI_API_KEY_HERE>",
    base_url="https://api.x.ai/v1",
)

response = client.responses.create(
    model="grok-4.7",
    input="Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return a[a.length/2]}",
)

print(response.output_text)
```

```
curl https://api.x.ai/v1/responses \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $XAI_API_KEY" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "model": "grok-4.7",
    "input": "Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return a[a.length/2]}"
  }'
```

```
import os
from xai_sdk import Client
from xai_sdk.chat import user

client = Client(api_key=os.getenv("XAI_API_KEY"))

chat = client.chat.create(model="grok-4.7")
chat.append(user("Fix this function and explain the bug: function median(a){a.sort();return a[a.length/2]}"))

print(chat.sample().content)
```

**标题**: Introducing Grok 4.7 | SpaceXSI

# Introducing Grok 4.7

SpaceXSI's most powerful model for coding and knowledge work. Twice as fast, at half the price of comparable models.

Grok 4.7 is our most capable model for coding and knowledge work. It works longer on difficult tasks, checks its own work more carefully, and comes with our best-calibrated safeguards to date. Served at the same price and speed as [Grok 4.6](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-6), it is highly competitive in its class.

CostTokensSteps

On CursorBench 4.0, which stresses longer-running coding tasks, Grok 4.7 is at the frontier in price-performance.

## [Model Improvements](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-7#model-improvements)

Grok 4.7 uses a new, larger base model compared to [Grok 4.6](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-6). It was trained with a longer reinforcement learning run on a harder mix of tasks, weighted toward problems that take many hours to complete. The model is better at verifying its own work and managing longer context. We also trained Grok 4.7 to natively understand the [Grok Bot](https://x.ai/bot) harness, making it better at conversational tasks and general knowledge work.

## [Safety & Cybersecurity](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-7#safety--cybersecurity)

Grok 4.7 was built with an entirely new safeguard stack. It is the strongest model we’ve tested on refusals and jailbreak resistance. In dual-use domains like cybersecurity and biological work, it leads on both utility for benign tasks and safe refusal on dangerous ones, topping LatchBio’s biosafety benchmark at 62.4%.

Grok 4.7 balances strong cyber defense capabilities with low refusal rates for legitimate use. It shows the highest safety on HackerBench v0.3, our benchmark for risky and malicious cyber tasks, allowing only 3.3% of risky dual-use prompts through while rarely blocking legitimate security work. We’ve also started giving select cybersecurity partners invite-only access to Grok 4.7’s red-team capabilities for defense research.

## [Pricing and availability](https://x.ai/news/grok-4-7#pricing-and-availability)

Grok 4.7 is available today in [Cursor](https://cursor.com) and [Grok Build](https://x.ai/build). It is also available through the [Grok API](https://console.x.ai?campaign=grok-4-7-blog&utm_source=website&utm_medium=referral&utm_campaign=grok-4-7-blog), third-party coding harnesses, and model routers and cloud platforms.

The model is priced starting at $2 per million input tokens and $6 per million output tokens. We also serve a fast variant with twice the output speed at twice the price.


===== https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25 =====
**标题**: Specification - Model Context Protocol

[Skip to main content](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#content-area)

# Specification

You are viewing an older version (2025-11-25) of the specification. [View the latest version (2026-07-28)](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/latest)

[Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) (MCP) is an open protocol that enables seamless integration between LLM applications and external data sources and tools. Whether you’re building an AI-powered IDE, enhancing a chat interface, or creating custom AI workflows, MCP provides a standardized way to connect LLMs with the context they need. This specification defines the authoritative protocol requirements, based on the TypeScript schema in [schema.ts](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/specification/blob/main/schema/2025-11-25/schema.ts). For implementation guides and examples, visit [modelcontextprotocol.io](https://modelcontextprotocol.io). The key words “MUST”, “MUST NOT”, “REQUIRED”, “SHALL”, “SHALL NOT”, “SHOULD”, “SHOULD NOT”, “RECOMMENDED”, “NOT RECOMMENDED”, “MAY”, and “OPTIONAL” in this document are to be interpreted as described in [BCP 14](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/bcp14) [[RFC2119](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2119)] [[RFC8174](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8174)] when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here.

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#overview) Overview

MCP provides a standardized way for applications to:

- Share contextual information with language models
- Expose tools and capabilities to AI systems
- Build composable integrations and workflows

The protocol uses [JSON-RPC](https://www.jsonrpc.org/) 2.0 messages to establish communication between:

- **Hosts**: LLM applications that initiate connections
- **Clients**: Connectors within the host application
- **Servers**: Services that provide context and capabilities

MCP takes some inspiration from the [Language Server Protocol](https://microsoft.github.io/language-server-protocol/), which standardizes how to add support for programming languages across a whole ecosystem of development tools. In a similar way, MCP standardizes how to integrate additional context and tools into the ecosystem of AI applications.

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#key-details) Key Details

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#base-protocol) Base Protocol

- [JSON-RPC](https://www.jsonrpc.org/) message format
- Stateful connections
- Server and client capability negotiation

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#features) Features

Servers offer any of the following features to clients:

- **Resources**: Context and data, for the user or the AI model to use
- **Prompts**: Templated messages and workflows for users
- **Tools**: Functions for the AI model to execute

Clients may offer the following features to servers:

- **Sampling**: Server-initiated agentic behaviors and recursive LLM interactions
- **Roots**: Server-initiated inquiries into URI or filesystem boundaries to operate in
- **Elicitation**: Server-initiated requests for additional information from users

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#additional-utilities) Additional Utilities

- Configuration
- Progress tracking
- Cancellation
- Error reporting
- Logging

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#security-and-trust-&-safety) Security and Trust & Safety

The Model Context Protocol enables powerful capabilities through arbitrary data access and code execution paths. With this power comes important security and trust considerations that all implementors must carefully address.

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#key-principles) Key Principles

1. **User Consent and Control**
   
   - Users must explicitly consent to and understand all data access and operations
   - Users must retain control over what data is shared and what actions are taken
   - Implementors should provide clear UIs for reviewing and authorizing activities
2. **Data Privacy**
   
   - Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before exposing user data to servers
   - Hosts must not transmit resource data elsewhere without user consent
   - User data should be protected with appropriate access controls
3. **Tool Safety**
   
   - Tools represent arbitrary code execution and must be treated with appropriate caution.
     
     - In particular, descriptions of tool behavior such as annotations should be considered untrusted, unless obtained from a trusted server.
   - Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before invoking any tool
   - Users should understand what each tool does before authorizing its use
4. **LLM Sampling Controls**
   
   - Users must explicitly approve any LLM sampling requests
   - Users should control:
     
     - Whether sampling occurs at all
     - The actual prompt that will be sent
     - What results the server can see
   - The protocol intentionally limits server visibility into prompts

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#implementation-guidelines) Implementation Guidelines

While MCP itself cannot enforce these security principles at the protocol level, implementors **SHOULD**:

1. Build robust consent and authorization flows into their applications
2. Provide clear documentation of security implications
3. Implement appropriate access controls and data protections
4. Follow security best practices in their integrations
5. Consider privacy implications in their feature designs

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25#learn-more) Learn More

Explore the detailed specification for each protocol component:


===== https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/latest =====
**标题**: Specification - Model Context Protocol

[Skip to main content](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#content-area)

# Specification

[Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) (MCP) is an open protocol that enables seamless integration between LLM applications and external data sources and tools. Whether you’re building an AI-powered IDE, enhancing a chat interface, or creating custom AI workflows, MCP provides a standardized way to connect LLMs with the context they need. This specification defines the authoritative protocol requirements, based on the TypeScript schema in [schema.ts](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/specification/blob/main/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts). For implementation guides and examples, visit [modelcontextprotocol.io](https://modelcontextprotocol.io). The key words “MUST”, “MUST NOT”, “REQUIRED”, “SHALL”, “SHALL NOT”, “SHOULD”, “SHOULD NOT”, “RECOMMENDED”, “NOT RECOMMENDED”, “MAY”, and “OPTIONAL” in this document are to be interpreted as described in [BCP 14](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/bcp14) [[RFC2119](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2119)] [[RFC8174](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8174)] when, and only when, they appear in all capitals, as shown here.

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#overview) Overview

MCP provides a standardized way for applications to:

- Share contextual information with language models
- Expose tools and capabilities to AI systems
- Build composable integrations and workflows

The protocol uses [JSON-RPC](https://www.jsonrpc.org/) 2.0 messages to establish communication between:

- **Hosts**: LLM applications that initiate connections
- **Clients**: Connectors within the host application
- **Servers**: Services that provide context and capabilities

MCP takes some inspiration from the [Language Server Protocol](https://microsoft.github.io/language-server-protocol/), which standardizes how to add support for programming languages across a whole ecosystem of development tools. In a similar way, MCP standardizes how to integrate additional context and tools into the ecosystem of AI applications.

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#key-details) Key Details

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#base-protocol) Base Protocol

- [JSON-RPC](https://www.jsonrpc.org/) message format
- Stateless, self-contained requests
- Per-request capability negotiation

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#features) Features

Servers offer any of the following features to clients:

- **Resources**: Context and data, for the user or the AI model to use
- **Prompts**: Templated messages and workflows for users
- **Tools**: Functions for the AI model to execute

Clients may offer the following features to servers:

- **Elicitation**: Server-initiated requests for additional information from users

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#additional-utilities) Additional Utilities

- Configuration
- Progress tracking
- Cancellation
- Error reporting

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#extensions) Extensions

Beyond the core protocol, MCP defines optional [extensions](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/overview) that add modular, specialized, or experimental functionality. Extensions are always opt-in and require explicit support from both client and server, negotiated during initialization. Notable extensions include:

- [**Tasks**](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/tasks/overview): Asynchronous execution of long-running operations, with polling, mid-flight input, and durable handles
- [**Skills over MCP**](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/community/working-groups/skills-over-mcp): Rich, structured instructions for agent workflows, discovered and consumed through MCP
- [**MCP Apps**](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/apps/overview): Interactive UI elements (charts, forms, video players) rendered inline within conversations

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#security-and-trust-&-safety) Security and Trust & Safety

The Model Context Protocol enables powerful capabilities through arbitrary data access and code execution paths. With this power comes important security and trust considerations that all implementors must carefully address.

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#key-principles) Key Principles

1. **User Consent and Control**
   
   - Users must explicitly consent to and understand all data access and operations
   - Users must retain control over what data is shared and what actions are taken
   - Implementors should provide clear UIs for reviewing and authorizing activities
2. **Data Privacy**
   
   - Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before exposing user data to servers
   - Hosts must not transmit resource data elsewhere without user consent
   - User data should be protected with appropriate access controls
3. **Tool Safety**
   
   - Tools represent arbitrary code execution and must be treated with appropriate caution.
     
     - In particular, descriptions of tool behavior such as annotations should be considered untrusted, unless obtained from a trusted server.
   - Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before invoking any tool
   - Users should understand what each tool does before authorizing its use

### [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#implementation-guidelines) Implementation Guidelines

While MCP itself cannot enforce these security principles at the protocol level, implementors **SHOULD**:

1. Build robust consent and authorization flows into their applications
2. Provide clear documentation of security implications
3. Implement appropriate access controls and data protections
4. Follow security best practices in their integrations
5. Consider privacy implications in their feature designs

## [​](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28#learn-more) Learn More

Explore the detailed specification for each protocol component:
