**标题**: Getting started - OpenClaw

On this pageWhat you need

First steps

# Getting started

Install OpenClaw, run onboarding, and chat with your AI assistant in about 5 minutes. By the end you will have a running Gateway, configured auth, and a working chat session.

## What you need

- **Node.js 24.16.0+ on 24.x, or 26.1.0+** (Node 26 is recommended; use 24.22.1+/26.12.1+ for the security fixes)
- **An existing Claude Code or Codex CLI login, or a provider API key** — onboarding can reuse it

**Tip**

Check your Node version with `node --version`. **Windows users:** the native Windows Hub app is the easiest desktop path. The PowerShell installer and WSL2 Gateway paths are also supported. See [Windows](https://docs.openclaw.ai/platforms/windows). Need to install Node? See [Node setup](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install/node).

## Try it in one command

bashCopy code

```bash
npx openclaw@latest
```

On a fresh install, choose **Quick start** after a one-line pointer to the [security guide](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security). That is the only onboarding prompt when usable AI access is already available: OpenClaw finds an existing Claude Code or Codex CLI login or API key, verifies it with a real completion, saves the config, and opens the web dashboard.

Quick start then asks **Keep OpenClaw running in the background?** Yes installs a copy of the same OpenClaw version under `~/.openclaw` and runs the Gateway as a background service from it, so clearing the npx cache does not stop it. No keeps the Gateway in this terminal until you press **Ctrl+C**; your config stays saved. If no detected route works, onboarding opens manual provider setup. Choose **Custom setup** to walk through all guided options instead.

To keep the Gateway running in the background later, install the CLI below and run `openclaw gateway install`, then `openclaw gateway start` to wait until it is ready. Run `openclaw` for the TUI or `openclaw dashboard` to reopen the web UI.

## Quick setup

### Install OpenClaw

### macOS / Linux

bashCopy code

```bash
curl -fsSL https://openclaw.ai/install.sh | bash
```

### Windows (PowerShell)

powershellCopy code

```powershell
iwr -useb https://openclaw.ai/install.ps1 | iex
```

**Note**

Other install methods (Docker, Nix, npm): [Install](https://docs.openclaw.ai/install).

### Complete onboarding

The installer starts the guided onboarding wizard automatically. Choose **Quick start** to reuse detected AI access and open the dashboard, or **Custom setup** for the full guided flow. Provider sign-in and optional setup can take longer. Return later with `openclaw configure` for additional settings. `openclaw onboard --classic` opens the classic step-by-step wizard instead.

See [Onboarding (CLI)](https://docs.openclaw.ai/start/wizard) for the full reference.

### Install the Gateway service

Quick start asks **Keep OpenClaw running in the background?** and installs the service when you accept, so skip this step in that case. Otherwise, press **Ctrl+C** to stop the foreground Gateway, then install the service and wait for it to be ready:

bashCopy code

```bash
openclaw gateway installopenclaw gateway start
```

This installs a LaunchAgent on macOS, a systemd user unit on Linux and WSL2, or a Scheduled Task on native Windows (with a per-user Startup-folder login item as the fallback if task creation is denied). Your config stays saved across the stop and the install.

`gateway install` returns once the service is registered, while the Gateway may still be starting. `gateway start` waits until the Gateway passes its health and readiness checks; it does not restart a Gateway that is already running.

### Verify the Gateway is running

bashCopy code

```bash
openclaw gateway status
```

You should see the Gateway listening on port 18789.

### Open the dashboard

bashCopy code

```bash
openclaw dashboard
```

This opens the Control UI in your browser. If it loads, everything is working.

### Send your first message

Type a message in the Control UI chat and you should get an AI reply.

Want to chat from your phone instead? The fastest channel to set up is [Telegram](https://docs.openclaw.ai/channels/telegram) (just a bot token). See [Channels](https://docs.openclaw.ai/channels) for all options.

Advanced: mount a custom Control UI build

If you maintain a localized or customized dashboard build, point `gateway.controlUi.root` to a directory that contains your built static assets and `index.html`.

bashCopy code

```bash
mkdir -p "$HOME/.openclaw/control-ui-custom"# Copy your built static files into that directory.
```

Then set:

jsonCopy code

```json
{  "gateway": {    "controlUi": {      "enabled": true,      "root": "${HOME}/.openclaw/control-ui-custom"    }  }}
```

Restart the gateway and reopen the dashboard:

bashCopy code

```bash
openclaw gateway restartopenclaw dashboard
```

## If setup does not work

One command turns the current state of your install into a diagnosis you can act on:

bashCopy code

```bash
openclaw triage
```

It runs read-only health checks, writes a sanitized prompt describing what it found, and then offers to hand that prompt to a coding agent it detects on your machine — Claude Code, Codex CLI, or the built-in OpenClaw agent — so the agent starts with the diagnosis already loaded. Pick "just print the commands" if you would rather run the handoff yourself.

Nothing leaves your machine until you choose an agent, and secrets, tokens, raw chat payloads, and raw logs are excluded from the prompt.

To read the findings yourself instead, run [`openclaw doctor`](https://docs.openclaw.ai/cli/doctor). For symptom-first routes, see [Troubleshooting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/help/troubleshooting).

If you run OpenClaw as a service account or want custom paths:

- `OPENCLAW_HOME` — home directory for internal path resolution
- `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR` — override the state directory
- `OPENCLAW_CONFIG_PATH` — override the config file path

Full reference: [Environment variables](https://docs.openclaw.ai/help/environment).

## 简要结论

基于提供的 `Security - OpenClaw` 页面内容，我找到的是一份**安全文档总览页/迁移索引页**，不是各安全子页面的正文。它能给出 OpenClaw 安全模型的框架、默认安全姿态、信任边界、主要风险域和维护者警告；但**没有展开完整的 threat model 细节、具体 CVE、已知事件、sandbox 实现细节或配置正文**。

**关键发现：**

- OpenClaw 默认姿态被描述为 `conservative defaults`：常规主机安装下 `Gateway` 绑定 `loopback`，未知 DM 默认走 `pairing code`，群组访问默认 `allowlisted` 且通常带 `mention gate`。
- 核心信任模型是 **one trust boundary per gateway**；OpenClaw 明确**不是**面向互不信任用户的 hostile multi-tenant 安全边界。
- 重要例外：`container images default to an exposed bind`，维护者要求 `pair that with auth`；部分 workspace channel（如 `ClickClack`）默认信任 workspace membership。
- 拥有 `message-tool` 权限的 `Agents` 默认可跨 conversations 和 channel providers 发消息；如需限制，要配置 `cross-provider messaging restrictions`。
- 页面提供 `openclaw security audit` 作为配置漂移检查入口，并列出 `openclaw security`、`openclaw policy` 等 CLI 工具。
- **未发现任何具体 known incidents 或 CVE 编号、漏洞公告、受影响版本、修复版本、事件日期；也没有完整 MITRE ATLAS 威胁映射细节。**

---

## 范围与完整性说明

页面明确指出 deeper details 在子页面中：

> The pages below are the deep end: the trust model, what the audit checks, and how to harden further as you expose more surface.

同时，页面后半部分是章节迁移索引：

> Every anchor this page used to publish still resolves here. Each entry below carries the original anchor and links to its new home.

因此，以下提取以**本页明确出现的文字、摘要、链接标题和锚点**为准；不会推断子页面未提供的内容。

本页没有展开的内容包括：

- 具体威胁清单、`Trust boundary matrix` 内容、`MITRE ATLAS` 技术映射；
- 每个 `checkId`、`severity`、`auto-fix support` 的明细；
- `Gateway WebSocket auth`、`Trusted proxy auth`、token/password/credential 的具体实现；
- `Sandboxing` 的具体机制或配置片段；
- 具体 prompt injection 攻击样例、绕过条件、模型评估细节；
- 任何具体 CVE、安全公告、历史事件或入侵案例。

---

# 完整安全模型提取（以本页可见内容为准）

## 1. Threat model / 信任模型

### 核心假设：one trust boundary per gateway

页面把安全模型限定为：

- 一个 `gateway` 对应一个受信边界；
- 边界可以是 `a single operator`，也可以是 `a team whose members trust each other`；
- `Group chats` 和 `multi-user` operation 被支持，但仅在该信任边界内部；
- `OpenClaw` **不是**为 `mutually adversarial users sharing one agent or gateway` 设计的 hostile multi-tenant security boundary；
- 对 `mixed-trust` 或 `adversarial-user` 场景，维护者要求拆分边界：`separate gateway + credentials`，最好是 `separate OS users or hosts`。

### 本页列出的 threat model 相关主题

- `Security trust model`：`One trust boundary per gateway, the boundary matrix, and the findings closed as no-action.`
- `Threat model`：`Adversarial threats to the OpenClaw platform and ClawHub, mapped to MITRE ATLAS.`
- 锚点包括：
  - `Scope: one trust boundary per gateway`
  - `Trust boundary matrix`
  - `Not vulnerabilities by design`
  - `Common findings closed as no-action`
  - `Gateway and node trust`
  - `Threat model`
  - `Reporting security issues`

**解读：** 维护者将一些安全发现归类为设计边界内行为或 `no-action`，但本页没有列出具体 findings。`Threat model` 具体内容在 `https://docs.openclaw.ai/security/THREAT-MODEL-ATLAS`，本页只提供摘要。

---

## 2. 默认网络暴露

| 项目 | 本页明确内容 |
|---|---|
| 常规主机安装 | `the Gateway binds to loopback` |
| 默认总体姿态 | `OpenClaw ships with conservative defaults` |
| 默认 DM 处理 | `most chat channels answer an unknown DM sender with a pairing code instead of processing the message` |
| 默认群组访问 | `group access is allowlisted, usually behind a mention gate` |
| 容器镜像例外 | `container images default to an exposed bind`，并要求 `pair that with auth` |
| workspace channel 例外 | 例如 `ClickClack` 默认信任 `workspace membership`；`each channel page states its exact defaults` |
| 暴露前检查 | `Gateway exposure runbook`：`Pre-flight and rollback checklist before exposing the Gateway beyond loopback` |
| 安全基线 | `Hardened baselines` 提供 `Copy/paste configs that keep the Gateway local, paired, and tool-restricted` |
| 漂移检测 | `openclaw security audit` 可判断是否偏离默认/安全状态 |

### 本页列出的 `Network exposure` 主题

- `Bind, firewall, discovery, Gateway auth, Tailscale, reverse proxy, dangerous flags`
- `Bind, port, firewall`
- `Docker port publishing with UFW`
- `mDNS/Bonjour discovery`
- `Gateway WebSocket auth`
- `Tailscale Serve identity headers`
- `Reverse proxy configuration`
- `HSTS and origin notes`
- `Control UI over HTTP`
- `Insecure/dangerous flags`
- `Flags tracked by the audit today`
- `All dangerous*/dangerously* keys in the config schema`

**未说明：** 本页没有给出具体 bind 地址、端口、firewall 规则、UFW 配置、reverse proxy 示例、HSTS 配置或危险 flag 的完整列表。

---

## 3. Authentication / 访问控制

### 默认认证与访问姿态

- 未知 DM 发送者默认不会被处理，而是收到 `pairing code`。
- 群组访问默认 `allowlisted`，通常还需 `mention gate`。
- `Hardened baselines` 的目标包括保持 `Gateway local, paired, and tool-restricted`。
- `Network exposure` 明确包含 `Gateway auth`。
- `Gateway WebSocket auth` 是网络暴露子主题。
- `Trusted proxy auth`：`Running the Gateway behind a reverse proxy that supplies the operator identity.`
- `Tailscale Serve identity headers` 被列为身份相关主题。
- `Rate limiting`：`Every Gateway rate limit: lockouts, throttles, caps, and cooldowns.`

### `Access control and allowlists` 主题

页面摘要：

- `DM policy, allowlists, DM session isolation, context visibility, command authorization.`

锚点包括：

- `DM access: pairing, allowlist, open, disabled`
- `Allowlists (two layers)`
- `DM session isolation (multi-user mode)`
- `Context visibility vs trigger authorization`
- `Command authorization`
- `Requester-scoped controls and prompt context`
- `Separate numbers (WhatsApp, Signal, Telegram)`
- `Secure baseline (copy/paste)`
- `Hardened baseline in 60 seconds`

**未说明：** 本页没有给出具体 token、password、OAuth、mTLS、session、cookie、header 或 operator identity 校验算法；也没有给出 `open`、`disabled` 等 DM policy 的配置细节。

## 4. Sandboxing 与 tool permissions

### 默认跨会话/跨 provider 消息能力

页面明确给出一个重要默认行为：拥有 `message-tool` 权限的 `Agents` **默认可以跨 conversations 和 channel providers 发消息**。如需把消息限制在当前 provider 或 conversation，需要配置 `cross-provider messaging restrictions`。

相关链接：`https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/tool-permissions#cross-provider-messaging`

## 5. Prompt injection risks

- `Prompt injection`：`Untrusted content reaching the model, model choice, and the wrapping that bounds it.`

锚点包括：

- `Prompt injection`
- `External content and untrusted-input wrapping`
- `Bypass flags (keep off in production)`

- `Requester-scoped controls and prompt context`
- `Context visibility vs trigger authorization`
- `Browser control risks` 与 `Browser SSRF policy (strict by default)` 共同构成真实浏览器/外部内容风险边界。

**风险含义：** 页面把 prompt injection 的核心描述为 `Untrusted content reaching the model`，并强调 `model choice` 和 `wrapping` 用于限定影响；`Bypass flags (keep off in production)` 明确提示生产环境不应开启绕过 flag。页面没有提供具体攻击示例、模型名单、wrapper 格式或 flag 名称。

# 维护者最重要警告（逐字原文）

以下引用均保持源内容原文；中文仅作简要说明。

## 1. 信任边界：最核心警告

> **Note**
>
> **One trust boundary per gateway.** This guidance assumes one trusted boundary per gateway: a single operator, or a team whose members trust each other. Group chats and [multi-user](https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/multi-user) operation are supported deployments inside that boundary. OpenClaw is not a hostile multi-tenant security boundary for mutually adversarial users sharing one agent or gateway. For mixed-trust or adversarial-user operation, split trust boundaries: separate gateway + credentials, ideally separate OS users or hosts.

**含义：** 这是全页最重要的安全边界声明。不要把同一个 `agent` 或 `gateway` 当作互不信任用户之间的强隔离边界。

## 2. 默认安全姿态

> OpenClaw ships with conservative defaults. On a regular host install the Gateway binds to loopback; most chat channels answer an unknown DM sender with a pairing code instead of processing the message; and group access is allowlisted, usually behind a mention gate.

**含义：** 常规主机安装默认本地绑定、DM pairing、群组 allowlist/mention gate。

## 3. 默认例外与必须配 auth 的警告

> The exceptions are deliberate and documented: container images default to an exposed bind (pair that with auth - see the [exposure runbook](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/exposure-runbook)), and a few workspace channels such as ClickClack trust workspace membership by default - each channel page states its exact defaults.

**含义：** 容器镜像是明确例外；`pair that with auth` 是维护者对暴露绑定的直接警告。`ClickClack` 等 channel 的默认信任模型需查看各 channel 页面。

## 4. 配置漂移检查

> Run on those defaults and you are in good shape, and one command tells you if you have drifted:

```bash
openclaw security audit
```

**含义：** 维护者建议保持在默认安全姿态，并用 `openclaw security audit` 检查是否偏离。

## 5. 跨 provider / conversation 消息风险

> Agents with message-tool access can send across conversations and channel providers by default. If your deployment needs messaging confined to the current provider or conversation, configure [cross-provider messaging restrictions](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/tool-permissions#cross-provider-messaging).

**含义：** `message-tool` 默认跨会话、跨 provider；如需限制必须显式配置。

## 6. 生产环境、危险配置与高风险标题

以下短句均来自本页章节/锚点标题，属于维护者给出的显式高风险提示：

> Bypass flags (keep off in production)

> Insecure/dangerous flags

> All dangerous*/dangerously* keys in the config schema

> Control UI over HTTP

> Full access (no sandbox)

> Browser SSRF policy (strict by default)

> Rotate (assume compromise if secrets leaked)

> Not vulnerabilities by design

> Common findings closed as no-action

**含义：** 页面在目录层面明确标出生产环境不应开启的 bypass flags、危险 flags、无 sandbox 的 full access、HTTP Control UI、SSRF 默认策略，以及疑似密钥泄露后的轮换假设。

---

## 最终判断

当前页面能提取出的“完整安全模型”是**框架级**：默认保守暴露、单 `gateway` 单信任边界、DM/群组访问控制、`Gateway auth`/proxy auth/identity headers、tool permissions 与 sandbox/read-only profiles、prompt injection 的 untrusted-input wrapping、审计与 incident response 流程、secrets/logs/storage/file/dependency 安全域。

但用户要求的若干“full detail”并不在本页正文内：具体 threat model、MITRE ATLAS 映射、`checkId` 目录、认证配置、sandbox 实现、prompt injection 细节、已知事件和 CVE 都需要继续读取下列子页面：

- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/trust-model`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/security/THREAT-MODEL-ATLAS`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/audit-checks`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/access-control`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/tool-permissions`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/prompt-injection`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/network-exposure`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/exposure-runbook`
- `https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/security/operator-incident-response`

## 简要结论

从提供的网页文本中，能可靠提取的主要是**仓库全名、页面标题/标语、README 可见标题、文件/目录列表和最新提交信息**。请求中的 **LICENSE 类型、primary language、star 数、硬件/系统要求、model providers（尤其 local）、具体安全警告、最新 release 版本/日期** 都未在该内容中明确显示，无法确认。

===== https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama =====
**标题**: Ollama - OpenClaw

On this pageWhere each section moved

Providers

# Ollama

OpenClaw talks to Ollama's native API (`/api/chat`), not the OpenAI-compatible `/v1` endpoint. Three setups are supported:

Setup What it uses Cloud + Local A reachable Ollama host, serving local models and (if signed in) `:cloud` models Local only A reachable Ollama host, local models only Ollama Cloud `https://ollama.com` directly through the `ollama-cloud` provider, no daemon

Hosted-only access is its own **Ollama Cloud** setup choice; see [Ollama Cloud](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama-cloud). Its `ollama-cloud/<model>` refs keep cloud routing separate from the local `ollama` provider.

**Warning**

Do not use the `/v1` OpenAI-compatible URL (`http://host:11434/v1`). It breaks tool calling and models can emit raw tool-call JSON as plain text. Use the native URL: `baseUrl: "http://host:11434"` (no `/v1`).

The current config key is `baseUrl`. `baseURL` is also accepted for OpenAI-SDK-style examples, but new config should use `baseUrl`.

This page is an index. Ollama is documented on nine pages, one per reader job. Open the page that matches your task.

Page Read it when [Ollama setup](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/setup) You are connecting an account: auth rules per host type, onboarding and manual setup, and the hybrid cloud-plus-local flow. [Ollama model discovery](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/model-discovery) You want to know how models are found: the implicit-discovery table, capability detection, and smoke-test checks. [Ollama node-local inference](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/node-local-inference) You are running models on a paired node: setup steps, model filtering, and direct verification commands. [Ollama vision and image description](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/vision) You are describing or understanding images through a local or hosted Ollama vision model. [Ollama configuration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/configuration) You are writing the provider entry: implicit discovery, an explicit model list, or a custom base URL. [Ollama config recipes](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/recipes) You want a working config to copy: per-scenario recipes, model selection, and quick verification. [Ollama Web Search](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/web-search) You are using Ollama as the `web_search` provider: host, auth, and requirement rules. [Ollama advanced configuration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/advanced) You are tuning behavior: context windows, thinking control, reasoning, costs, embeddings, and streaming. [Ollama troubleshooting](https://docs.openclaw.ai/providers/ollama/troubleshooting) Something is broken: detection, connection, tool-JSON, garbled output, timeouts, and WSL2 crash loops.

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13657 =====
**标题**: [2503.13657] Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail?
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 17 Mar 2025 ( v1 ), last revised 26 Oct 2025 (this version, v3)]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13657#content)

# Title:Why Do Multi-Agent LLM Systems Fail?

Authors:[Mert Cemri](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Cemri%2C%20M), [Melissa Z. Pan](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Pan%2C%20M%20Z), [Shuyi Yang](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Yang%2C%20S), [Lakshya A. Agrawal](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Agrawal%2C%20L%20A), [Bhavya Chopra](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Chopra%2C%20B), [Rishabh Tiwari](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Tiwari%2C%20R), [Kurt Keutzer](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Keutzer%2C%20K), [Aditya Parameswaran](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Parameswaran%2C%20A), [Dan Klein](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Klein%2C%20D), [Kannan Ramchandran](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Ramchandran%2C%20K), [Matei Zaharia](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Zaharia%2C%20M), [Joseph E. Gonzalez](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Gonzalez%2C%20J%20E), [Ion Stoica](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Stoica%2C%20I)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.13657) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.13657v3)

> Abstract:Despite enthusiasm for Multi-Agent LLM Systems (MAS), their performance gains on popular benchmarks are often minimal. This gap highlights a critical need for a principled understanding of why MAS fail. Addressing this question requires systematic identification and analysis of failure patterns. We introduce MAST-Data, a comprehensive dataset of 1600+ annotated traces collected across 7 popular MAS frameworks. MAST-Data is the first multi-agent system dataset to outline the failure dynamics in MAS for guiding the development of better future systems. To enable systematic classification of failures for MAST-Data, we build the first Multi-Agent System Failure Taxonomy (MAST). We develop MAST through rigorous analysis of 150 traces, guided closely by expert human annotators and validated by high inter-annotator agreement (kappa = 0.88). This process identifies 14 unique modes, clustered into 3 categories: (i) system design issues, (ii) inter-agent misalignment, and (iii) task verification. To enable scalable annotation, we develop an LLM-as-a-Judge pipeline with high agreement with human annotations. We leverage MAST and MAST-Data to analyze failure patterns across models (GPT4, Claude 3, Qwen2.5, CodeLlama) and tasks (coding, math, general agent), demonstrating improvement headrooms from better MAS design. Our analysis provides insights revealing that identified failures require more sophisticated solutions, highlighting a clear roadmap for future research. We publicly release our comprehensive dataset (MAST-Data), the MAST, and our LLM annotator to facilitate widespread research and development in MAS.

Comments: ArXiv v3 Subjects: Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI) Cite as: [arXiv:2503.13657](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13657) [cs.AI]   (or [arXiv:2503.13657v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13657v3) [cs.AI] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2503.13657](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2503.13657)

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629 =====
**标题**: [2210.03629] ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 6 Oct 2022 ( v1 ), last revised 10 Mar 2023 (this version, v3)]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629#content)

# Title:ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models

Authors:[Shunyu Yao](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Yao%2C%20S), [Jeffrey Zhao](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Zhao%2C%20J), [Dian Yu](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Yu%2C%20D), [Nan Du](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Du%2C%20N), [Izhak Shafran](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Shafran%2C%20I), [Karthik Narasimhan](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Narasimhan%2C%20K), [Yuan Cao](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Cao%2C%20Y)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.03629) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2210.03629v3)

> Abstract:While large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across tasks in language understanding and interactive decision making, their abilities for reasoning (e.g. chain-of-thought prompting) and acting (e.g. action plan generation) have primarily been studied as separate topics. In this paper, we explore the use of LLMs to generate both reasoning traces and task-specific actions in an interleaved manner, allowing for greater synergy between the two: reasoning traces help the model induce, track, and update action plans as well as handle exceptions, while actions allow it to interface with external sources, such as knowledge bases or environments, to gather additional information. We apply our approach, named ReAct, to a diverse set of language and decision making tasks and demonstrate its effectiveness over state-of-the-art baselines, as well as improved human interpretability and trustworthiness over methods without reasoning or acting components. Concretely, on question answering (HotpotQA) and fact verification (Fever), ReAct overcomes issues of hallucination and error propagation prevalent in chain-of-thought reasoning by interacting with a simple Wikipedia API, and generates human-like task-solving trajectories that are more interpretable than baselines without reasoning traces. On two interactive decision making benchmarks (ALFWorld and WebShop), ReAct outperforms imitation and reinforcement learning methods by an absolute success rate of 34% and 10% respectively, while being prompted with only one or two in-context examples. Project site with code: [this https URL](https://react-lm.github.io)

Comments: v3 is the ICLR camera ready version with some typos fixed. Project site with code: [this https URL](https://react-lm.github.io) Subjects: Computation and Language (cs.CL); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Machine Learning (cs.LG) Cite as: [arXiv:2210.03629](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629) [cs.CL]   (or [arXiv:2210.03629v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629v3) [cs.CL] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2210.03629](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2210.03629)

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045 =====
**标题**: [2406.12045] $τ$-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 17 Jun 2024]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045#content)

# Title:τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains

Authors:[Shunyu Yao](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Yao%2C%20S), [Noah Shinn](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Shinn%2C%20N), [Pedram Razavi](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Razavi%2C%20P), [Karthik Narasimhan](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Narasimhan%2C%20K)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.12045) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.12045v1)

> Abstract:Existing benchmarks do not test language agents on their interaction with human users or ability to follow domain-specific rules, both of which are vital for deploying them in real world applications. We propose τ-bench, a benchmark emulating dynamic conversations between a user (simulated by language models) and a language agent provided with domain-specific API tools and policy guidelines. We employ an efficient and faithful evaluation process that compares the database state at the end of a conversation with the annotated goal state. We also propose a new metric (pass^k) to evaluate the reliability of agent behavior over multiple trials. Our experiments show that even state-of-the-art function calling agents (like gpt-4o) succeed on <50% of the tasks, and are quite inconsistent (pass^8 <25% in retail). Our findings point to the need for methods that can improve the ability of agents to act consistently and follow rules reliably.

Subjects: Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computation and Language (cs.CL) Cite as: [arXiv:2406.12045](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045) [cs.AI]   (or [arXiv:2406.12045v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045v1) [cs.AI] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2406.12045](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2406.12045)

## Submission history

===== https://arxiv.org/search/?query=Berkeley+Function+Calling+Leaderboard&searchtype=all&abstracts=show&order=-announced_date_first&size=50 =====
## 简要结论

在提供的 arXiv 搜索结果页中，页面显示 `Showing 1–30 of 30 results for all: Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard`。我逐条核对了 30 个结果的题名，**没有找到**题名为：

> `The Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL): From Tool Use to Agentic Evaluation of Large Language Models`

的论文。因此：

- **按精确标题匹配：0 条**
- **本页无法提取该目标论文的 exact arXiv ID、authors、submission date 或 abstract snippet**
- 下面列出的是该页面中**全部 30 条搜索命中结果**，它们多为使用、扩展、审计或评测 BFCL 的论文，均不是目标题名。

> 说明：arXiv 页面中的 `Function\-Calling` 等 TeX 转义按页面原样保留；代码内文本为页面原文片段，代码外的 `…` 表示截断。提交日期仅列页面中的 `Submitted` / `v1 submitted`，未列 `originally announced`。作者按页面显示列出；页面标注未显示的附加作者以 `et al.` 保留。

---

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22138 =====
**标题**: [2511.22138] TinyLLM: Evaluation and Optimization of Small Language Models for Agentic Tasks on Edge Devices
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 27 Nov 2025]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22138#content)

# Title:TinyLLM: Evaluation and Optimization of Small Language Models for Agentic Tasks on Edge Devices

Authors:[Mohd Ariful Haque](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Haque%2C%20M%20A) (1), [Fahad Rahman](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Rahman%2C%20F) (2), [Kishor Datta Gupta](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Gupta%2C%20K%20D) (1), [Khalil Shujaee](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Shujaee%2C%20K) (1), [Roy George](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=George%2C%20R) (1) ((1) Clark Atlanta University, (2) United International University)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.22138) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.22138v1)

> Abstract:This paper investigates the effectiveness of small language models (SLMs) for agentic tasks (function/tool/API calling) with a focus on running agents on edge devices without reliance on cloud infrastructure. We evaluate SLMs using the Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL) framework and describe parameter-driven optimization strategies that include supervised fine-tuning (SFT), parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT), reinforcement learning (RL)-based optimization, preference alignment via Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), and hybrid methods. We report results for models including TinyAgent, TinyLlama, Qwen, and xLAM across BFCL categories (simple, multiple, parallel, parallel-multiple, and relevance detection), both in live and non-live settings, and in multi-turn evaluations. We additionally detail a DPO training pipeline constructed from AgentBank data (e.g., ALFRED), including our conversion of SFT data to chosen-rejected pairs using TinyLlama responses as rejected outputs and manual validation. Our results demonstrate clear accuracy differences across model scales where medium-sized models (1-3B parameters) significantly outperform ultra-compact models (<1B parameters), achieving up to 65.74% overall accuracy, and 55.62% multi-turn accuracy with hybrid optimization. This study highlights the importance of hybrid optimization strategies that enable small language models to deliver accurate, efficient, and stable agentic AI on edge devices, making privacy-preserving, low-latency autonomous agents practical beyond the cloud.

Comments: 8 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables Subjects: Machine Learning (cs.LG) Cite as: [arXiv:2511.22138](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22138) [cs.LG]   (or [arXiv:2511.22138v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22138v1) [cs.LG] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2511.22138](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2511.22138)

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00914 =====
**标题**: [2504.00914] On the Robustness of Agentic Function Calling
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 1 Apr 2025]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00914#content)

# Title:On the Robustness of Agentic Function Calling

Authors:[Ella Rabinovich](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Rabinovich%2C%20E), [Ateret Anaby-Tavor](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Anaby-Tavor%2C%20A)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.00914) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.00914v1)

> Abstract:Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly acting as autonomous agents, with function calling (FC) capabilities enabling them to invoke specific tools for tasks. While prior research has primarily focused on improving FC accuracy, little attention has been given to the robustness of these agents to perturbations in their input. We introduce a benchmark assessing FC robustness in two key areas: resilience to naturalistic query variations, and stability in function calling when the toolkit expands with semantically related tools. Evaluating best-performing FC models on a carefully expanded subset of the Berkeley function calling leaderboard (BFCL), we identify critical weaknesses in existing evaluation methodologies, and highlight areas for improvement in real-world agentic deployments.

Comments: 7 pages, TrustNLP@NAACL25 Subjects: Computation and Language (cs.CL) Cite as: [arXiv:2504.00914](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00914) [cs.CL]   (or [arXiv:2504.00914v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00914v1) [cs.CL] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2504.00914](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2504.00914)

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35381 =====
**标题**: [2609.35381] MCP Error Messages Written for Developers Hurt the Most Capable Agents Most
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 28 Sep 2026 ( v1 ), last revised 29 Sep 2026 (this version, v2)]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35381#content)

# Title:MCP Error Messages Written for Developers Hurt the Most Capable Agents Most

Authors:[Xiaonan Xu](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Xu%2C%20X), [Wenjing Wu](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Wu%2C%20W)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.35381) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.35381v2)

> Abstract:Many Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers wrap web APIs built for human developers, and their error messages tell the reader to run a command, edit a configuration, open a web page or wait. Many agents that read them can only call the server's tools. In 150 widely used MCP servers, 949 of 3,001 error messages tell the caller what to do next, and half of these steps depend on something the server cannot see about the caller. On credential errors, 62 of 67 steps ask for a terminal command, a configuration change or a web page; on rate limits, 20 of 30 say to wait and retry without naming the call to repeat. We tested five OpenAI models that act only through the tools of Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard tasks, and the agents did what the step said. On expired credentials, a terminal command in the step left 45% of tasks recovered, and the loss it caused grew from 18 points for GPT-5.5 to 69 for GPT-6 Astra. On a rate limit, GitHub's "Wait before retrying." left 6%. We tested two remedies. For MCP developers, naming a server tool in the step raised recovery on expired credentials to 84%, with the login tool in place of the command, and on a rate limit to 88%, with the call to repeat in place of the bare wait. For agent developers, deleting the step with a one-sentence prompt before the model reads it raised recovery on expired credentials to 82%.

Comments: 15 pages, 6 tables. Submitted to the Journal of Systems and Software. Data and code: [this https URL](https://github.com/WenJing95/tool-error-text) Subjects: Software Engineering (cs.SE); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI) Cite as: [arXiv:2609.35381](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35381) [cs.SE]   (or [arXiv:2609.35381v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35381v2) [cs.SE] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2609.35381](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2609.35381)

===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20994 =====
**标题**: [2604.20994] Breaking MCP with Function Hijacking Attacks: Novel Threats for Function Calling and Agentic Models
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 22 Apr 2026 ( v1 ), last revised 31 Aug 2026 (this version, v2)]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20994#content)

# Title:Breaking MCP with Function Hijacking Attacks: Novel Threats for Function Calling and Agentic Models

Authors:[Yannis Belkhiter](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Belkhiter%2C%20Y), [Giulio Zizzo](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Zizzo%2C%20G), [Sergio Maffeis](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Maffeis%2C%20S), [Seshu Tirupathi](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Tirupathi%2C%20S), [John D. Kelleher](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Kelleher%2C%20J%20D)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.20994) [HTML (experimental)](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.20994v2)

> Abstract:The growth of agentic AI has drawn significant attention to function calling Large Language Models (LLMs), which are designed to extend the capabilities of AI-powered system by invoking external functions. Injection and jailbreaking attacks have been extensively explored to showcase the vulnerabilities of LLMs to user prompt manipulation. The expanded capabilities of agentic models introduce further vulnerabilities via their function calling interface. Recent work in LLM security showed that function calling can be abused, leading to data tampering and theft, causing disruptive behavior such as endless loops, or causing LLMs to produce harmful content in the style of jailbreaking attacks. This paper introduces a novel function hijacking attack (FHA) that manipulates the tool selection process of agentic models to force the invocation of an attacker-chosen function. While existing attacks focus on semantic preference of the model for function-calling tasks, we show that FHA is largely agnostic to the context semantics and remains effective across domains and function sets. We demonstrate that FHA generalizes to unseen queries and payload perturbations under a fixed target model, reaching 62.5% to 81.9% ASR on held-out queries across 4 function-calling LLMs (instructed and reasoning models), evaluated on the Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL). We further evaluate the cross-model transferability of FHA, showing that FHA can be transferred to other model sizes and families (11.2-27.6% ASR). Our findings further demonstrate the need for strong guardrails and modules for agentic systems.

Comments: Accepted to TMLR (08/26) Subjects: Cryptography and Security (cs.CR); Artificial Intelligence (cs.AI); Computation and Language (cs.CL) Cite as: [arXiv:2604.20994](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20994) [cs.CR]   (or [arXiv:2604.20994v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20994v2) [cs.CR] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2604.20994](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2604.20994)

Focus to learn more

Use this model

- Libraries
- [Transformers](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b?library=transformers)
  
  How to use openai/gpt-oss-20b with Transformers:
  
  ```
  # Use a pipeline as a high-level helper
  from transformers import pipeline
  
  pipe = pipeline("text-generation", model="openai/gpt-oss-20b")
  messages = [
      {"role": "user", "content": "Who are you?"},
  ]
  pipe(messages)
  ```
  
  ```
  # pip install -U transformers accelerate
  # Load model directly
  from transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModelForCausalLM
  
  tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained("openai/gpt-oss-20b")
  model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained("openai/gpt-oss-20b", device_map="auto")
  messages = [
      {"role": "user", "content": "Who are you?"},
  ]
  inputs = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(
  	messages,
  	add_generation_prompt=True,
  	tokenize=True,
  	return_dict=True,
  	return_tensors="pt",
  ).to(model.device)
  
  outputs = model.generate(**inputs, max_new_tokens=256)
  print(tokenizer.decode(outputs[0][inputs["input_ids"].shape[-1]:]))
  ```

![gpt-oss-20b](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/gpt-oss/main/docs/gpt-oss-20b.svg)

[**Try gpt-oss**](https://gpt-oss.com) · [**Guides**](https://cookbook.openai.com/topic/gpt-oss) · [**Model card**](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10925) · [**OpenAI blog**](https://openai.com/index/introducing-gpt-oss/)

Welcome to the gpt-oss series, [OpenAI’s open-weight models](https://openai.com/open-models) designed for powerful reasoning, agentic tasks, and versatile developer use cases.

We’re releasing two flavors of these open models:

- `gpt-oss-120b` — for production, general purpose, high reasoning use cases that fit into a single 80GB GPU (like NVIDIA H100 or AMD MI300X) (117B parameters with 5.1B active parameters)
- `gpt-oss-20b` — for lower latency, and local or specialized use cases (21B parameters with 3.6B active parameters)

Both models were trained on our [harmony response format](https://github.com/openai/harmony) and should only be used with the harmony format as it will not work correctly otherwise.

> This model card is dedicated to the smaller `gpt-oss-20b` model. Check out [`gpt-oss-120b`](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-120b) for the larger model.

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#highlights)Highlights

- **Permissive Apache 2.0 license:** Build freely without copyleft restrictions or patent risk—ideal for experimentation, customization, and commercial deployment.
- **Configurable reasoning effort:** Easily adjust the reasoning effort (low, medium, high) based on your specific use case and latency needs.
- **Full chain-of-thought:** Gain complete access to the model’s reasoning process, facilitating easier debugging and increased trust in outputs. It’s not intended to be shown to end users.
- **Fine-tunable:** Fully customize models to your specific use case through parameter fine-tuning.
- **Agentic capabilities:** Use the models’ native capabilities for function calling, [web browsing](https://github.com/openai/gpt-oss/tree/main?tab=readme-ov-file#browser), [Python code execution](https://github.com/openai/gpt-oss/tree/main?tab=readme-ov-file#python), and Structured Outputs.
- **MXFP4 quantization:** The models were post-trained with MXFP4 quantization of the MoE weights, making `gpt-oss-120b` run on a single 80GB GPU (like NVIDIA H100 or AMD MI300X) and the `gpt-oss-20b` model run within 16GB of memory. All evals were performed with the same MXFP4 quantization.

* * *

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#inference-examples)Inference examples

## [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#transformers)Transformers

You can use `gpt-oss-120b` and `gpt-oss-20b` with Transformers. If you use the Transformers chat template, it will automatically apply the [harmony response format](https://github.com/openai/harmony). If you use `model.generate` directly, you need to apply the harmony format manually using the chat template or use our [openai-harmony](https://github.com/openai/harmony) package.

To get started, install the necessary dependencies to setup your environment:

```
pip install -U transformers kernels torch 
```

Once, setup you can proceed to run the model by running the snippet below:

```py
from transformers import pipeline
import torch

model_id = "openai/gpt-oss-20b"

pipe = pipeline(
    "text-generation",
    model=model_id,
    torch_dtype="auto",
    device_map="auto",
)

messages = [
    {"role": "user", "content": "Explain quantum mechanics clearly and concisely."},
]

outputs = pipe(
    messages,
    max_new_tokens=256,
)
print(outputs[0]["generated_text"][-1])
```

Alternatively, you can run the model via [`Transformers Serve`](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main/serving) to spin up a OpenAI-compatible webserver:

```
transformers serve
transformers chat localhost:8000 --model-name-or-path openai/gpt-oss-20b
```

[Learn more about how to use gpt-oss with Transformers.](https://cookbook.openai.com/articles/gpt-oss/run-transformers)

## [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#vllm)vLLM

vLLM recommends using [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/) for Python dependency management. You can use vLLM to spin up an OpenAI-compatible webserver. The following command will automatically download the model and start the server.

```bash
uv pip install --pre vllm==0.10.1+gptoss \
    --extra-index-url https://wheels.vllm.ai/gpt-oss/ \
    --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/nightly/cu128 \
    --index-strategy unsafe-best-match

vllm serve openai/gpt-oss-20b
```

[Learn more about how to use gpt-oss with vLLM.](https://cookbook.openai.com/articles/gpt-oss/run-vllm)

## [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#pytorch--triton)PyTorch / Triton

To learn about how to use this model with PyTorch and Triton, check out our [reference implementations in the gpt-oss repository](https://github.com/openai/gpt-oss?tab=readme-ov-file#reference-pytorch-implementation).

## [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#ollama)Ollama

If you are trying to run gpt-oss on consumer hardware, you can use Ollama by running the following commands after [installing Ollama](https://ollama.com/download).

```bash
# gpt-oss-20b
ollama pull gpt-oss:20b
ollama run gpt-oss:20b
```

[Learn more about how to use gpt-oss with Ollama.](https://cookbook.openai.com/articles/gpt-oss/run-locally-ollama)

#### [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#lm-studio)LM Studio

If you are using [LM Studio](https://lmstudio.ai/) you can use the following commands to download.

```bash
lms get openai/gpt-oss-20b
```

Check out our [awesome list](https://github.com/openai/gpt-oss/blob/main/awesome-gpt-oss.md) for a broader collection of gpt-oss resources and inference partners.

* * *

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#download-the-model)Download the model

You can download the model weights from the [Hugging Face Hub](https://huggingface.co/collections/openai/gpt-oss-68911959590a1634ba11c7a4) directly from Hugging Face CLI:

```shell
huggingface-cli download openai/gpt-oss-20b --include "original/*" --local-dir gpt-oss-20b/
pip install gpt-oss
python -m gpt_oss.chat model/
```

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#reasoning-levels)Reasoning levels

You can adjust the reasoning level that suits your task across three levels:

- **Low:** Fast responses for general dialogue.
- **Medium:** Balanced speed and detail.
- **High:** Deep and detailed analysis.

The reasoning level can be set in the system prompts, e.g., "Reasoning: high".

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#tool-use)Tool use

The gpt-oss models are excellent for:

- Web browsing (using built-in browsing tools)
- Function calling with defined schemas
- Agentic operations like browser tasks

# [](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-20b#fine-tuning)Fine-tuning

Both gpt-oss models can be fine-tuned for a variety of specialized use cases.

This smaller model `gpt-oss-20b` can be fine-tuned on consumer hardware, whereas the larger [`gpt-oss-120b`](https://huggingface.co/openai/gpt-oss-120b) can be fine-tuned on a single H100 node.

===== https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview =====
**标题**: Grok Bot | SpaceXSI Docs

# [Grok Bot](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#grok-bot-1)

Copy for LLM[View as Markdown](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview.md)

Grok Bot gives you Bots you can keep around: AI teammates with names, jobs, and context that compounds over time. Each Bot works on a persistent cloud computer with a browser, filesystem, and terminal, so tasks finish in your real tools instead of as chat drafts. [Get started](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/get-started) with your first Bot, or read [how teams roll it out](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/teams-and-enterprises).

You work with a Bot by messaging it. Type, dictate, or start a voice chat. Give it a task, the relevant context, and access to the tools it needs. It takes on multi-step work across apps and websites, keeps you updated in the conversation, and comes back when something needs your approval.

Grok Bot runs on macOS, Windows, Linux, iOS, and Android. On The X Subscription, it's included with Premium, Super, and Ultra, for individuals and team seats. On a legacy Cursor plan, it's included with every paid individual plan and with Cursor Teams. You can also link an individual SuperGrok, SuperGrok Plus, SuperGrok Heavy, or X Premium+ subscription, or sign in with a seat on a self-serve Grok Business plan. See [Grok Bot on The X Subscription](https://docs.x.ai/x-subscription/grok-bot) and [Plans and billing](https://cursor.com/help/grok-bot/plans) for the full matrix.

## [What makes Grok Bot different](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#what-makes-grok-bot-different)

- **Each Bot has a computer.** Your Bots run on a persistent cloud computer with a browser, filesystem, and terminal. They use [connected apps](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/computer-and-apps#connect-an-app) where available and computer use for everything else, and work continues while your laptop is closed.
- **Setup is a message, not a workflow builder.** Create a Bot, describe the job, and grant access as it asks. The same Bot is reachable from the desktop app and the [iPhone and Android apps](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/mobile).
- **Bots coordinate with each other.** Your Bots can run in parallel, message each other, share context in [group chats](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/chat-and-collaboration#start-a-group-chat), and pass ownership of a task, so you aren't the router between tools.
- **Bots learn workflows from demonstration.** Walk a Bot through a multi-step path once, and it can save the path as a [skill](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/skills-routines-and-automations) and rerun it on a schedule.
- **Context compounds.** A named Bot keeps its memory, files, browser sessions, and preferences across sessions instead of resetting on every task.

## [Your Bots share one computer](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#your-bots-share-one-computer)

All of your Bots use the same cloud computer, sharing its files, browser sessions, and app logins, which makes handoffs work without repeating setup. The computer belongs to your account, not to an individual Bot, so treat anything placed on it as available to every Bot you run; between users, isolation is strict. [The computer and apps](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/computer-and-apps) covers the full model. When you chat with a teammate's [shared Bot](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/shared-bots), it usually works on your computer too.

## [A good first handoff](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#a-good-first-handoff)

Start with a real task that spans a few tools and has a clear finish line:

> Pull this week's pipeline review list from our CRM. Skip anyone already in an active sequence. Research the top five accounts across the web and Slack, pull the right contacts, draft outreach in my voice, and leave me drafts to approve by tomorrow morning.

This tells the Bot what to do, where to work, what context to pull in, and what finished work looks like. Review the result, correct it, and turn the stable process into a [skill or routine](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/skills-routines-and-automations).

## [Tag @bot on X](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#tag-bot-on-x)

You can also start work from X. Tag [@bot](https://x.com/bot) in a post or reply, and your main Bot picks up the post as a task while @bot confirms with a short public reply. Tagging requires an X account connected to your Grok account and is not available in every region yet. [Tag @bot on X](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/tag-on-x) covers setup, regional availability, and why @bot might not reply.

## [FAQ](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#faq)

### [Does Grok Bot keep working when my laptop is closed?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#does-grok-bot-keep-working-when-my-laptop-is-closed)

Yes. Work runs on the cloud computer. Closing the app, your laptop, or your phone doesn't stop a background turn or a routine.

### [Can several Bots work at the same time?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#can-several-bots-work-at-the-same-time)

Yes. Bots can reason, use connected apps, work with files, and coordinate in parallel. Each Bot gets its own screen on the shared computer, and one Bot runs one computer-use task on its screen at a time.

### [What does a Bot remember?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#what-does-a-bot-remember)

Stable preferences, role context, and summaries of prior work. Conversations and learned context stay separate per Bot, except for a few facts about you, such as your name and time zone, that all your Bots share. Shared files, browser sessions, and direct handoffs move context between them. For consequential decisions, ask the Bot to check the current source instead of relying on memory. See [What a Bot remembers](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/bots#what-a-bot-remembers).

### [Can a Bot use any website?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#can-a-bot-use-any-website)

Bots can use many browser-based tools, including services without a connected app. A site can still block automation, expire a session, or require a human step. The Bot hands those steps to you rather than working around them.

### [Which platforms are supported?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#which-platforms-are-supported)

The desktop app runs on macOS (Apple silicon and Intel), Windows (x64 and Arm64), and Linux (x64 and Arm64), and the mobile app runs on iPhone and Android. The computers Bots work on run in Cursor's cloud.

### [How much does Grok Bot cost?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#how-much-does-grok-bot-cost)

On The X Subscription, Grok Bot is included with Premium, Super, and Ultra and draws from the same weekly usage pool as Cursor and the Grok apps. On a legacy Cursor plan, access comes with every paid individual plan and with Cursor Teams, through a linked individual SuperGrok or X Premium+ subscription, or with a seat on a self-serve Grok Business plan. Usage resets weekly. See [How much does Grok Bot cost?](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/faq#how-much-does-grok-bot-cost).

## [Next steps](https://docs.x.ai/grok-bot/overview#next-steps)


===== https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02595 =====
**标题**: [2607.02595] Attributing Structured-Output Gains in Function Calling: Interface Alignment versus Procedural Transfer
**发布时间**: [Submitted on 1 Jul 2026 ( v1 ), last revised 25 Aug 2026 (this version, v2)]

[Skip to main content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02595#content)

# Title:Attributing Structured-Output Gains in Function Calling: Interface Alignment versus Procedural Transfer

Authors:[Wanyi Chen](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Chen%2C%20W), [Daoyuan Chen](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Chen%2C%20D), [Fang Kong](https://arxiv.org/search/cs?searchtype=author&query=Kong%2C%20F)

[View PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.02595)

> Abstract:Structured-output benchmarks reward both task decisions and interface compliance, so prompt-induced function-calling gains require attribution before they can be interpreted as transferable skill. We introduce a four-layer gain-attribution protocol for prompt-prepended skill injection, combining canonicalized rescoring, format-only controls, repaired/balanced induction, and portability checks. Applied to the Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL) and scoped with API-Bank, MATH-500, and MultiHop-RAG, the protocol shows that several apparent gains are better attributed to interface alignment than to procedural transfer: format-only prompts match or exceed full skills in key BFCL cells, repaired/balanced induction removes the largest sub-frontier gains, and API-Bank target-native gains are matched within 0.5 percentage points (pp) by length-matched generic procedural prompts. These findings treat format compliance as a useful engineering capability while clarifying what a structured-output score certifies. We release BFCL-CANONICAL and recommend canonicalized metrics, balanced induction, and format-only baselines for function-calling skill-gain attribution. Code and data are available at [this https URL](https://github.com/couragec/skill-injection-attribution).

Comments: 18 pages, 5 figures Subjects: Software Engineering (cs.SE); Programming Languages (cs.PL) Cite as: [arXiv:2607.02595](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02595) [cs.SE]   (or [arXiv:2607.02595v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02595v2) [cs.SE] for this version)   [https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2607.02595](https://doi.org/10.48550/arXiv.2607.02595)

Focus to learn more

arXiv-issued DOI via DataCite

## Quick start

A few options to get `llama.cpp` installed on your machine:

```bash
# curl
curl -LsSf https://llama.app/install.sh | sh

# powershell
irm https://llama.app/install.ps1 | iex
```

- Visit https://llama.app and follow the instructions
- Run with Docker - see our [Docker documentation](docs/docker.md)
- Download pre-built binaries from the [releases page](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases)
- Build from source by cloning this repository - check out [our build guide](docs/build.md)

Once installed:

```sh
# Download and run a model directly from Hugging Face
llama cli -hf ggml-org/Qwen3.5-0.8B-GGUF

# Launch OpenAI-compatible API server
llama serve -hf ggml-org/Qwen3.5-0.8B-GGUF
```

## Description

The main goal of `llama.cpp` is to enable LLM (and VLM) inference with minimal setup and state-of-the-art performance on
a wide range of hardware - locally and in the cloud.

- Plain C/C++ implementation without any dependencies
- Apple silicon is a first-class citizen - optimized via ARM NEON, Accelerate and Metal frameworks
- AVX, AVX2, AVX512 and AMX support for x86 architectures
- RVV, ZVFH, ZFH, ZICBOP and ZIHINTPAUSE support for RISC-V architectures
- 1.5-bit, 2-bit, 3-bit, 4-bit, 5-bit, 6-bit, and 8-bit integer quantization for faster inference and reduced memory use
- Custom CUDA kernels for running LLMs on NVIDIA GPUs (support for AMD GPUs via HIP and Moore Threads GPUs via MUSA)
- Vulkan and SYCL backend support
- CPU+GPU hybrid inference to partially accelerate models larger than the total VRAM capacity

The `llama.cpp` project is build on top of the [ggml](https://github.com/ggml-org/ggml) library.

===== https://gorilla.cs.berkeley.edu/leaderboard.html =====
## 简要结论

基于提供的网页内容，当前页面是 **Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL) V4**，可见最后更新日期为 **2026-04-12**（原文：**"Last Updated: 2026-04-12 [Change Log]"**）。页面将 BFCL 描述为评估 LLM 准确调用函数/工具的能力，并已从早期工具调用评测扩展到 **multi-turn** 和 **holistic agentic evaluation**。  
需要特别注意：页面明确说明了 **AST** 评测与 **Overall Accuracy** 的计算方式，但在提供的内容中 **没有直接展开说明 "executable" 或 "state-based" 评测细节**，这些信息被指向外链博客。

---

## 1. 当前页与版本演进（V3/V4）

页面主标题和说明：

> "Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard (BFCL) V4 evaluates the LLM's ability to call functions (aka tools) accurately."

并说明：

> "This leaderboard consists of real-world data and will be updated periodically."

版本线在页面中按博客链接列出：

| 版本 | 页面中的说明 | 含义 |
|---|---|---|
| BFCL-v1 | "introducing AST as an evaluation metric" | 引入 **AST** 作为函数_call 评测指标 |
| BFCL-v2 | "introducing enterprise and OSS-contributed functions" | 引入企业场景与 OSS 贡献的函数 |
| **BFCL-v3** | "introducing multi-turn interactions" | 引入 **多轮交互** 任务 |
| **BFCL-v4** | "introducing holistic agentic evaluation" | 引入更整体的 **Agentic** 评测；表头中可见 "Web Search"、"Memory" 等类别 |

因此，当前 V4 榜单并不是只测单次函数调用，而是保留了早期 AST/单轮类别，并叠加 V3 的多轮与 V4 的 agentic/web/memory 方向。

---

## 2. 可见的任务/指标类别

表格表头在抓取文本中被压平成一行，但可识别出的类别与列包括：

| 大类/表头词 | 可见子列或相关列 | 说明 |
|---|---|---|
| "Agentic" | "Web Search"、"Memory" | V4 的 agentic 方向；表头同时列出 Web Search 与 Memory |
| "Multi Turn" | "Multi turn" | 对应 V3 引入的 multi-turn interactions |
| "Single Turn" | "Non-live (AST)"、"Live (AST)" | 单轮函数调用，明确使用 AST 指标 |
| "Hallucination Measurement" | "Relevance"、"Irrelevance" | 幻觉/相关性测量 |
| "Format Sensitivity" | "Max Delta"、"SD" | 格式敏感性；页面特别说明只支持 Prompt 模型 |
| "Latency (s)" | "Mean" | 延迟指标，单位为秒 |
| 其他列 | "Rank 🔼"、"Overall Acc"、"Model"、"Cost ($)"、"Organization"、"License" | 排名、准确率、成本、组织、许可证等 |

此外，页面还有 **"Wagon Wheel"** 图表，说明为：

> "The following chart shows the comparison of the models based on a few metrics."

以及一个 **"Function Calling Demo"**，用于展示输入 prompt/function 后的输出格式；该 demo 不是排行榜评分方法本身。

---

## 3. 评测方法：AST / executable / state-based

### 页面中明确可见的内容

- **AST 是明确写出的评测指标**：  
  > "BFCL-v1 ... introducing AST as an evaluation metric"

- 表头中仍可见：  
  **"Non-live (AST)"**、**"Live (AST)"**

- 总体分数说明：  
  > "**Overall Accuracy** is the **unweighted** average of all the sub-categories."

- 详细组成指向外链：  
  > "For details on score composition, please refer to our blog"  
  该链接指向 `https://gorilla.cs.berkeley.edu/blogs/13_bfcl_v3_multi_turn.html#leaderboard-composition`。

### 关于 executable / state-based

提供的网页正文中 **没有出现对 "executable evaluation" 或 "state-based evaluation" 的明确描述**，也没有说明哪些 V3/V4 子任务采用可执行环境检查或状态检查。页面只给出：

- "BFCL-v3 ... introducing multi-turn interactions"
- "BFCL-v4 ... introducing holistic agentic evaluation"
- "For more information on the evaluation dataset and methodology, please refer to our blogs"

并外链到：

- `https://gorilla.cs.berkeley.edu/blogs/13_bfcl_v3_multi_turn.html`
- `https://gorilla.cs.berkeley.edu/blogs/15_bfcl_v4_web_search.html`
- `https://github.com/ShishirPatil/gorilla/tree/main/berkeley-function-call-leaderboard`

因此，**仅依据该页面可确认的是 AST、Non-live/Live AST、多轮、Web Search、Memory、幻觉测量、格式敏感性和延迟等指标；不能直接确认 executable/state-based 的具体实现细节**。

---

## 4. 模型如何提交 / 如何复现

页面没有显示自动上传表单或 API 提交流程；给出的方式是联系维护者：

> "If you would like to add your model or contribute test-cases, please contact us via [discord](https://discord.gg/grXXvj9Whz)."

复现与版本信息：

- "Models are evaluated using commit **f7cf735**."
- "All the model response we obtained is available [here](https://github.com/HuanzhiMao/BFCL-Result)."
- 复现方式：
  - checkout 指定 checkpoint：`f7cf7359b7ac615a0b294831c5ba2bc95ee4a000`
  - 或安装：`pip install bfcl-eval==2025.12.17`

模型在榜中还按调用模式区分：

> "**FC** = native support for function/tool calling. **Prompt** = walk-around for function calling, using model's normal text generation capability."

表中同一模型常出现多个条目，例如 `GPT-5.2-2025-12-11 (FC)` 与 `GPT-5.2-2025-12-11 (Prompt)`，说明比较时要注意 FC/Prompt 变体不同。

---

## 5. 是否出现 open-weight / local 模型

**是，榜单中出现大量开放权重或可下载模型条目**，尤其是带有 Hugging Face 链接和开放/半开放许可证的模型。但页面本身没有使用 "local" 标签，也没有明确说明这些模型是否在本地运行。

可见例子包括：

- `GLM-4.6 (FC thinking)` — `MIT`
- `Moonshotai-Kimi-K2-Instruct (FC)` — `modified-mit`
- `DeepSeek-V3.2-Exp ...` — `MIT`
- `Qwen3-*` 系列 — `apache-2.0`
- `xLAM-2-*` 系列 — `cc-by-nc-4.0`
- `Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (FC)` / `Llama-4-*` — `Meta Llama 3 Community` / `Meta Llama 4 Community`
- `Gemma-3-*` — `gemma-terms-of-use`
- `Granite-*` — `Apache-2.0`
- `ToolACE-2-8B (FC)` — `Apache-2.0`
- `Llama-3.1-Nemotron-Ultra-253B-v1 (FC)` — `nvidia-open-model-license`

同时也大量存在 `Proprietary` 模型，如 Anthropic Claude、OpenAI GPT/o-series、Google Gemini、xAI Grok、Mistral AI、Amazon Nova 等。  
需要注意：部分 "开放" 许可证带有非商业、社区或研究限制，如 `cc-by-nc-4.0`、`gemma-terms-of-use`、`Meta Llama Community`、`Mistral AI Research License`，不等同于完全无限制开源。

---

## 6. 分数解释方面的明确注意事项

页面明确给出的 caveats 包括：

1. **Overall Accuracy 是未加权平均**  
   > "**Overall Accuracy** is the **unweighted** average of all the sub-categories."  
   这意味着各子类别等权计入，而不是按难度、样本量或真实使用频率加权。

2. **详细 score composition 未在主页展开**  
   > "For details on score composition, please refer to our blog"  
   因此看总分时需要参考外链博客了解组成。

3. **Format Sensitivity 只适用于 Prompt 模型**  
   > "**Format sensitivity** test cases are only supported for prompt (non-FC) models."  
   表格中 FC 模型相关列常见 `N/A`，比较 FC 与 Prompt 模型时要注意这一点。

4. **FC 与 Prompt 不是同一种调用方式**  
   > "**FC** = native support for function/tool calling. **Prompt** = walk-around for function calling, using model's normal text generation capability."

5. **Cost 和 Latency 是单独指标，不是准确率**  
   > "**Cost** is calculated as an estimate of the cost for the entire benchmark, in USD. **Latency** is measured in seconds."

6. **榜单会更新，且结果绑定到特定代码版本**  
   > "This leaderboard consists of real-world data and will be updated periodically."  
   并且：  
   > "Models are evaluated using commit **f7cf735**."

---

**总结**：当前 BFCL V4 在页面中被呈现为一个从 "function/tool calling" 扩展到 "holistic agentic evaluation" 的综合榜；明确可见的评测基础包括 AST、单轮/多轮、Web Search、Memory、幻觉测量、格式敏感性、成本与延迟。关于用户特别问到的 **executable/state-based**，该提供页面没有直接说明，只把方法论和 score composition 指向外部博客与代码仓库。


===== https://x.ai/news/grok-4-1 =====
## 简要结论

该页面是对 **Grok 4.1** 的发布与能力说明。**关键发现**：页面明确给出可用平台、Auto mode / model picker 上线方式、静默灰度评估、LMArena 排名与 Elo、EQ-Bench3 / Creative Writing v3 / hallucination / FActScore 等评估方法；但**未显示明确发布日期**，也**未发现任何 open weights / 开源权重 / 权重下载声明**。EQ-Bench、Creative Writing v3、Hallucination Rate、FActScore 在提供文本中只有方法或图表标题，**未给出具体数值分数**。

## 1. 精确标题与发布日期

- model card 链接中的日期：`2025-11-17`，不是发布日期声明：

- 示例回答中的年份，也不是发布日期：

## 2. 可用性与发布范围

**提取要点**

- 面向 **all users** 上线。
- 平台：`grok.com`、`𝕏`、`iOS and Android apps`。
- 在 `Auto mode` 中立即 rollout。
- 可在 `model picker` 中显式选择 `“Grok 4.1”`。
- 提供 Grok.com / iOS / Android 入口链接。
- 静默灰度覆盖 `grok.com, X, and mobile apps` 的 production traffic。

**原文逐字引用**

> "Grok 4.1 is now available to all users on grok.com, 𝕏, and the iOS and Android apps. It is rolling out immediately in Auto mode and can be selected explicitly as “Grok 4.1” in the model picker."

> "We conducted a gradual silent rollout of preliminary Grok 4.1 builds to a progressively larger share of production traffic across grok.com, X, and mobile apps. During the two-week silent rollout we ran continuous blind pairwise evaluations on live traffic."

**入口链接**

- `Open Grok.com`：`https://grok.com/?referrer=website`
- `Open Grok on iOS`：`https://apps.apple.com/us/app/grok/id6670324846`
- `Open Grok on Android`：`https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=ai.x.grok`

---

## 3. 模型改进与能力声明

**提取要点**

- 声称带来 `real-world usability` 的显著改进。
- 强调 `creative, emotional, and collaborative interactions`。
- 更擅长理解 `nuanced intent`，对话更有吸引力，人格更连贯。
- 保留前代的 `razor-sharp intelligence and reliability`。
- 声称在 blind human preference evaluations 中建立新标准。
- 后训练重点是降低信息查询场景中的事实幻觉。

**原文逐字引用**

> "We are excited to introduce Grok 4.1, which brings significant improvements to the real-world usability of Grok. Our 4.1 model is exceptionally capable in creative, emotional, and collaborative interactions. It is more perceptive to nuanced intent, compelling to speak with, and coherent in personality, while fully retaining the razor-sharp intelligence and reliability of its predecessors."

> "Grok 4.1 establishes a new standard in blind human preference evaluations."

> "Fast (non-reasoning) models equipped with search tools deliver quick answers, but they can be vulnerable to factual errors due to constrained reasoning depth and limited tool-call budgets."

> "In Grok 4.1 post-training, we focus on reducing factual hallucinations for information-seeking prompts. Subsequently we have observed significant reductions in hallucination rate for sampled production info-seeking prompts."

---

## 4. 训练方法、奖励模型与 agentic / 工具使用相关声明

**提取要点**

- 使用与 Grok 4 相同的 `large scale reinforcement learning infrastructure`。
- 优化目标包括 `style, personality, helpfulness, and alignment`。
- 针对 `non-verifiable reward signals` 开发新方法。
- 使用 `frontier agentic reasoning models as reward models`，自主评估并迭代回答。
- 评估 hallucination 时使用 `web search tools` 的 `non-reasoning model`。
- `non-reasoning mode` 使用 `no thinking tokens` 立即响应。
- 页面没有明确声明 Grok 4.1 对终端用户开放通用 agentic 工具调用能力；相关表述主要是训练中的 reward models 和评估中的 search tools。

**原文逐字引用**

> "To achieve this, we used the same large scale reinforcement learning infrastructure that powered Grok 4 and applied it to optimize the style, personality, helpfulness, and alignment of the model. In order to optimize these non-verifiable reward signals, we developed new methods that let us use frontier agentic reasoning models as reward models to autonomously evaluate and iterate on responses at scale."

> "Grok 4.1 in its non-reasoning mode (code name: `tensor`) uses no thinking tokens for an immediate response and ranks #2 at 1465 Elo."

> "The evaluations were done by evaluating non-reasoning model with web search tools"

> "Fast (non-reasoning) models equipped with search tools deliver quick answers, but they can be vulnerable to factual errors due to constrained reasoning depth and limited tool-call budgets."

**示例中的工具/行动相关句子（来自 Previous Grok 示例，不是 Grok 4.1 技术声明）**

> "Want me to check X for real-time visitor feedback or generate a map of these spots?"

## 5. 基准、数字与方法声明

### 5.1 可见数字与结果汇总

| 类别 | 可见数字 / 结果 | 说明 |
|---|---:|---|
| 静默灰度偏好评估 | `64.78%`；`two-week`；`November 1–14, 2025` | Grok 4.1 相比 previous production model 在 traffic 中被偏好 |
| LMArena Text Leaderboard | `Overall Style Control Elo 13251525` | 原文图表文字未分隔，按原样记录；不作为明确排名分数解释 |
| LMArena Text Arena | `#1`；`1483 Elo`；`31 points` | `Grok 4.1 Thinking` / `quasarflux` 排名 #1，领先最高非 xAI 模型 31 分 |
| LMArena non-reasoning | `#2`；`1465 Elo` | `Grok 4.1` / `tensor`，使用 `no thinking tokens` |
| Grok 4 对比 | `#33` | 页面称 Grok 4 的 overall rank 为 #33 |
| EQ-Bench3 | `45`；`3 turns`；`Claude Sonnet 3.7` | 45 个 roleplay scenarios，多数为 3 turns；judge 为 Claude Sonnet 3.7；未给出具体分数 |
| Creative Writing v3 | `32`；`3 iterations` | 32 个 writing prompts，3 iterations；未给出具体分数 |
| FActScore | `500 biography questions` | 公共基准，由 500 个传记问题组成 |
| Hallucination Rate / FActScore | `Lower score is better` | 两个指标均标注越低越好；未给出具体数值降幅或分数 |

### 5.2 关键原文逐字引用

**静默灰度与偏好率**

> "Compared to the previous production model in traffic, Grok 4.1 is preferred 64.78% of the time."

**LMArena Text Arena**

> "In LMArena's [Text Arena](https://lmarena.ai/leaderboard/text), Grok 4.1 Thinking (code name: `quasarflux`) holds the #1 overall position with 1483 Elo —a commanding margin of 31 points over the highest non-xAI model. Grok 4.1 in its non-reasoning mode (code name: `tensor`) uses no thinking tokens for an immediate response and ranks #2 at 1465 Elo. Grok 4.1 non-thinking surpasses every other model’s full-reasoning configuration on the public leaderboard. Grok 4.1 significantly surpasses Grok 4, which had an overall rank of #33."

**EQ-Bench3 / EQ-Bench 方法**

> "To measure progress on our model’s personality and interpersonal ability, we evaluated Grok 4.1 on [EQ-Bench3](https://eqbench.com/). EQ-Bench is a LLM-judged test, evaluating active emotional intelligence abilities, understanding, insight, empathy, and interpersonal skills. The test set contains 45 challenging roleplay scenarios, most of which constitute pre-written prompts spanning 3 turns. The benchmark evaluates the performance of the models by validating the models’ responses against several criteria. Additionally, the benchmark conducts pairwise comparisons to report a normalized Elo computation for each model in the leaderboard."

> "We report the rubric score and normalized Elo score by running the [official benchmark repository](https://github.com/EQ-bench/eqbench3). The scores were computed with the default sampling parameters, prescribed judge (Claude Sonnet 3.7), and no system prompt in accordance with the benchmark."

**Creative Writing v3 方法**

> "We also measured the performance of 4.1 models on the [Creative Writing v3 benchmark](https://eqbench.com/creative_writing.html). In this benchmark, models generate responses to 32 distinct writing prompts across 3 iterations. Similar to EQ-Bench, scores are computed using both rubrics and model battle normalized Elo."

> "For EQ-Bench3 and Creative Writing v3, we are working with the author(s) to get the number on the leaderboard."

**Hallucination Rate / FActScore 方法**

> "We evaluate hallucination rate on a stratified sample of real-world information-seeking queries from production traffic. We also evaluate FActScore, which is a public benchmark consisting of 500 biography questions on individuals."

> "Hallucination Rate"

> "Lower score is better"

> "FActScore"

> "Lower score is better"

> "The evaluations were done by evaluating non-reasoning model with web search tools"

> "Hallucination rate defined as macro-average of percentage of atomic claims with major/minor errors over model responses"

**图表标题 / 标签**

- `LMArena Text Leaderboard`
- `Overall Style Control Elo 13251525`
- `EQ-Bench`
- `Emotional Intelligence Benchmark - Elo (Normalized)`
- `Creative Writing v3`
- `Judging creative writing reliably - Elo (Normalized)`
